# Numbers and facts for 1A: checkpoint bands, strengths and stores, the calls, start loyalty (research lane 1.10, 2026-11-16)

Status: first pass for milestone 1A (decision 0073, FOUNDATION section 12, row 1.10). Ten research helpers read primary pages; this note is the synthesis, and the appendices (A to L) hold their cited evidence unedited except for a title line. **Nothing here is pack data.** The draft CSV is `packs/union-1861/source/drafts/oob/strengths-stores-1861.csv`. The repository's data files were read and never edited.

## How to read this note

- **Bands, not targets.** Each checkpoint below is a band with the sources that bound it, set before any run and never fitted from one. A count of planned operations is the record's cases, never a required event.
- **Quality words:** `return` is a dated official count; `estimate` a stated estimate; `inferred` a reading by the helper or the lead's lane; `snippet-unverified` was seen only in a search summary or an unreachable page and carries no number.
- **Fetch caveat.** The fetch tool paraphrases and the Official Records were mostly read through civilwar.com page transcriptions, spirit61.info and archive.org OCR. Page numbers are the site's labels and can drift by a page. **Re-read each figure in print before it enters pack data.** The helpers marked what they could not check.
- **Banned and weak sources.** Davis's *Rise and Fall* and J. E. Johnston's *Narrative* are not cited as evidence (0074). Memoirs are never a sole source. Wikipedia is never evidence: two ship strengths that a helper found only on Wikipedia infoboxes are left unverified and carry no figure.
- **Blocked and not circumvented:** census.gov and HathiTrust (by rule), the Library of Congress, usmcu.edu (403), the NHHC DANFS pages (404 or refused), muse.jhu.edu, core.ac.uk, several state pages (403), the Seward Papers at Rochester (403). Each is listed in the appendix that hit it.
- **Two search-result pages carried embedded instructions** that a helper (N9b) ignored. No other page tried to direct a helper.

## Corrections to the brief and to `OPEN_QUESTIONS`

These are things the helpers found that differ from what the brief or the filed leads said. The lead has accepted the first.
1. **OR I-1 pp. 368 to 394 are not a stores table.** They are the middle of Chapter IV, "Operations in Florida": Harvey Brown's relief expedition for Fort Pickens, 3 to 25 April 1861. The "about forty blocks" was lane 0.3's list of blocks with no stores found (accepted by the lead).
2. **The 400,000-volunteer act is dated 8 August 1861**, not 8 May. The 8 May act has no number; its title is "to raise an additional military force to serve during the war" (OR IV-1 p. 302; JCCS p. 201).
3. **Walker's call of 8 March for 8,000 men is not found.** His 9 March letter lists places totalling 11,700 (OR IV-1 p. 135). The "8,000" per state is the 3,000 of 8 April plus the 5,000 of 16 April calls.
4. **The 6 May act recognises the war and authorises letters of marque**; it is not a separate declaration (OR IV-1 p. 282; JCCS p. 188).
5. **Letcher's transfer proclamation is dated 6 June** (OR I-2 p. 912), published in General Orders 25 of 8 June (p. 911).
6. **The Union quota is 94 regiments and 73,391 men** (OR III-1 p. 69). The figures 73,910 and 72,540 in the research notes were not found in the OR.
7. **Harney's relief is not a conflict:** Special Orders 135 are dated 16 May, he received them on the evening of 30 May and Lyon took over on 31 May (OR I-3 pp. 374, 381).
8. **The Lyon "November 1861" letter** is 23 November 1860, to the Manhattan *Express* (*Last Political Writings*, pp. 25 to 26).
9. **Anderson's relief estimate is 20,000** (OR I-1 p. 197); the 70,000 appears only in a jfepperson.org enclosure transcription that contradicts that page's own quotation of Holt.
10. **The habeas order is 27 April 1861** (OR I-51 pt 1 p. 337; N&H 2 p. 39); no source gives 17 April.
11. **Campbell's documents are in OR I-53 pp. 162 to 163**, not OR I-1 pp. 280 to 295.
12. **The Bull Run triple "460 / 1,124 / 1,312"** was not found in either of its supposed sources; the Union missing figure is 1,216 (OR I-2 p. 327; Livermore p. 77).

## Priority 1: the 31 July 1861 checkpoint bands

### Which states have seceded (the band is exact)

Eleven: South Carolina (20 Dec 1860), Mississippi (9 Jan), Florida (10 Jan), Alabama (11 Jan), Georgia (19 Jan), Louisiana (26 Jan), Texas (1 Feb, popular vote 23 Feb, effective 2 Mar), Virginia (17 Apr, ratified 23 May), Arkansas (6 May), North Carolina (20 May), Tennessee (declaration 6 May, popular vote 8 June). Confederate admission is by Statute for each (the six original states 8 Feb; Texas 2 Mar; Virginia 7 May; Arkansas 20 May; North Carolina and Tennessee admitted "on a condition" 17 May, the completing act's date not found). Missouri and Kentucky, and Maryland and Delaware, **had not seceded** on 31 July; the Confederacy admitted Missouri (20 Aug and 28 Nov) and Kentucky (10 Dec) after the window. Sources and conflicts (Alabama's printed 61-89, Texas's votes, North Carolina's 20 or 21 May): Appendix A. The popular tallies for the Deep South come from a secondary (Appleton's *Annual Cyclopaedia*) and need a primary.

### The side of each government whose side could change

| government | band on 31 July | key dated acts and sources |
|---|---|---|
| Maryland | Union; not seceded; Hicks governor; Federal troops at Baltimore from 13 May; legislature declared no power to secede (29 Apr, vote counts conflict: 53-12 snippet vs AAC) | MSA exhibit; OR I-2 p. 29 |
| Delaware | Union; not seceded; Burton governor; legislature rejected Mississippi's overture (3 Jan) | AAC |
| Kentucky | neutral by the Governor's proclamation of 20 May; legislature Union-majority; government not seceded | AAC; Kentucky archives (House resolution 16 May vs "17th") |
| Missouri | Union by 31 July: the convention vacated the offices (30 or 31 July) and installed Gamble's provisional government; Jackson's rival government continues in the field | Missouri convention journal (March volume); MoSA; the July journal not found |
| Western Virginia | Union: Restored Government of Virginia at Wheeling, Pierpont governor from 20 June; senators seated 13 July | WV Archives |
| Creek, Choctaw, Chickasaw | allied to the Confederacy by treaty (10 and 12 July) | CSAL |
| Cherokee | neutral (Ross's proclamation, 17 May); treaty 7 October is after the window | OR I-13 p. 490 |
| Seminole, Osage, Quapaw, others | not yet; treaties from 1 August are after the window | CSAL |

Union posts left in the Indian Territory: Fort Smith 23 April; Fort Washita about 15 to 16 April; Fort Arbuckle 3 May (Texans in 5 May); Fort Cobb before 9 May (OR I-1 pp. 648 to 650). The Confederacy's Act for the protection of certain Indian tribes (approved 21 May) precedes every treaty and is an obvious scenario hook (Appendix A, findings).

### Whether Washington is held

**Yes, throughout.** It was isolated from 19 to 25 April (the Baltimore riot, bridges burned); Arlington Heights and Alexandria were occupied on 24 May; Bull Run was lost on 21 July and the capital was not attacked. The Department of Washington had about 41,000 present on 30 June (OR III-1 p. 301). Sources: OR I-2 pp. 29 to 30, 38 to 40, 314 to 316 (Appendix A).

### Each side's men under arms on 31 July

No dated return for 31 July exists in the sources read, so these are bands with the sources that bound them.

| side and measure | value | source and quality |
|---|---|---|
| Union, partial abstract of about 30 June | 152,354 aggregate; 108,440 present for duty | OR III-1 p. 301, **return, not confirmed**: it omits Annapolis, Kentucky, part of Ohio and unattached men and recruits; the printed total (129,894) and the summed total (147,736) disagree, and the reviewer's OCR reads the totals row differently (about 162,864 aggregate and 108,100 present; Washington 41,100 not 41,000). Re-read in print. |
| Union, 1 July | **186,751 on the rolls** (present and absent) | Livermore p. 47, column "No. on Union Rolls" (the Provost Marshal General series), **return**. The "183,588 present" figure given in the first draft is dropped: it is not on Livermore pp. 42 to 47 (it may be Fox's; unverified). |
| Union, "force at command", 1 July | about 310,000 (OR III-1 p. 304) or 307,875 (AAC): includes about 50,000 to 60,000 in 55 accepted regiments and 25,000 regulars not yet raised | return and secondary; **the p. 304 figure is not confirmed** (the reviewer's OCR is garbled) |
| **Union, 31 July, on the rolls (working bracket)** | **about 145,000 to 245,000 on the rolls**; present for duty is lower | **inferred, from two flows** (arithmetic below). **Entering:** the 55 regiments the AAC says were accepted and entered service within about 20 days of 1 July, about 50,000 to 60,000 men (secondary). **Leaving:** the three-months men credited on the 15 April call, 77,875 (the OR III-1 p. 699 rows summed) to 91,816 (the p. 69 footnote); the April-mustered regiments' terms ran out roughly 18 to 30 July (Appendix F: the dates rest on mirrors citing Dyer and Bates), Patterson wrote on 17 July that the terms expired "within seven days" and on 21 July that five regiments had gone home and four more were leaving the next day (OR I-2), and McDowell reports the 4th Pennsylvania and the 8th New York battery leaving on the eve of Bull Run. **Lower edge:** 186,751 + 50,000 - 91,816 = about 145,000 (every three-months man gone, entries at the low end, none of the departed re-enlisted into the entering regiments: a floor, not an expectation). **Upper edge:** 186,751 + 60,000 = about 247,000, rounded to 245,000 (none gone). A middle case (60,000 entering, 77,875 gone) gives about 169,000. The 30 June partial abstract shows present for duty at about two-thirds of aggregate (108,100 to 108,440 against about 152,000 to 163,000: unconfirmed OCR), so a present-for-duty reading of this bracket would be roughly 100,000 to 165,000, **an inference lane 1.14 must not score against the rolls bracket as if the measures were the same**. **For lane 1.14: until a primary 31 July return is found, a run is not failed for falling below the lower edge.** |
| Confederate, Bull Run armies, 21 July | 32,232 effectives (33,752 present for duty) | Livermore pp. 42, 77, **return/estimate** |
| Confederate, whole, July 1861 | about 112,040 | Livermore p. 42, **estimate** that he says is probably too low |
| Confederate units accepted, about 20 to 29 July | 190 regiments and 34 battalions | Walker's report, OR IV-1 pp. 496 to 498: a **unit count, not a headcount** |
| Navy | 42 vessels in commission 4 March, 7,600 complement | Soley; "22,000 seamen for December" unverified; Marines not found |

**The Confederate band has no sourced upper bound.** Walker's and Cooper's reports (OR IV-1) are the place to look; the helper read parts of them but found no dated total. The 112,040 figure and the 190 regiments are the two bounds in hand. The lead should set the upper band from them and the regiments' typical size once a primary figure is found.

### Planned operations fought (a count, not a required event)

Twenty-six cases between 5 March and 31 July, each with date, commanders, who planned it, the plan's OR page and strengths and losses where read (Appendix B). Counts by definition:

| definition | Union-planned | Confederate-planned | disputed | total |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Livermore-scale (strengths and losses given): First Bull Run only | 1 | 0 | 0 | **1** |
| NPS principal battles in the window (Sumter, Sewell's Point, Aquia Creek, Big Bethel, Philippi, Hoke's Run, Boonville, Carthage, Rich Mountain, Blackburn's Ford, Manassas I) | 8 or 9 | 1 (Sumter) | 1 (Carthage) | **11** |
| Eight ground-troop battles with a stated plan (excluding the three tiny naval exchanges and the reconnaissance) | 6 | 1 | 1 | **8** |
| Battle plus planned skirmish or affair | about 20 | 3 (Sumter, Cole Camp inferred, Mesilla) | 1 | about **24** |

The Confederate side planned almost nothing beyond the Sumter bombardment (12 April) and Baylor's New Mexico stroke (23 to 27 July); where a Confederate commander "planned" something, it was a counter-plan on the day (Beauregard at Bull Run, Magruder at Big Bethel, Jackson at Hoke's Run). The count therefore does not require a Confederate planned operation, and the lead should say so in the band. Disputed: Carthage (Sigel advanced to meet the State Guard; CWSAC says Jackson induced the attack).

## Pushback cases, S01 to S21: the lower bounds

N3 traced every pushback in S01 to S21 to a primary page where it could (Appendix C). 34 of 41 rows are verified (27 of 34 catalogue rows, and all 7 extras); 7 catalogue rows are note-only. Types are the game's: objection to an order (OBJ-O) or to a plan (OBJ-P), refusal (REF), slow walk (SW), relay objection or hold (RELAY), filter hold or trim (FILTER), compliance with reservations (CR).

| type | verified catalogue cases | all catalogue cases | verified with extras |
|---|---|---|---|
| objection to a plan | 10 | 13 | 11 |
| refusal | 10 | 11 | 13 |
| slow walk | 2 | 3 | 2 |
| relay objection or hold | 2 | 2 | 2 |
| filter hold or trim | 2 | 3 | 3 |
| compliance with reservations | 1 | 2 | 2 |
| objection to an order | 0 | 0 | 1 |

Dated verified primaries include: Adams's refusal of the Pickens order (letters of 1 April; 31 March is the date Vogdes received the order: ORN I-4 pp. 109 to 110), Porter and the *Powhatan* (ORN I-4 pp. 112, 237 to 240), the governors' refusals of the call (OR III-1 pp. 70, 72, 76, 82 to 83, 91 to 92, 99; Harris's is a 20 April letter, and the "not a single man" telegram is only on a blog), Patterson's three cases, Harney's agreement with Price (OR I-3 pp. 374 to 376), Hicks and Brown, Cadwalader, Seward's two papers and Scott's 28 March memorandum. **Not verified:** Burlingame ch. 22 (failed to load twice; the source for the Scott 5 March reading and Summers), McCauley's burning of Gosport (ORN pp. 272 to 313 unreachable; own report plus a museum page), McDowell's "warning" (only his post-battle report) and Cameron's refusals to Congress (a popular secondary).

**Judgement calls the lead should know:** Harney's agreement with Price is labelled CR and Scott's slow walk SW; case N3-11 could be SW or RELAY. One person's refusal and the same event's relays are counted separately. The counts are lower bounds; the upper bounds are the lead's.

## Priority 2: strengths and stores

### What the helpers found

- **Stores: six dated figures** for Union blocks (OR I-1 and ORN I-4, read directly in archive.org OCR):

  | block | stores_days | date | basis | source |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `fb_us_sumter_garrison` | 7 (band 4 to 10) | 1 Apr 1861 | stated, "about one week longer" if the laborers go | OR I-1 p. 230 (also bread 4 to 5 days on 3 Apr, p. 232) |
  | `fb_us_key_west_garrison` | 120 | 15 Jan 1861 | stated four months, x30 | OR I-1 p. 343 (Brannan to Deas) |
  | `fb_us_pickens_garrison` | 150 | 5 Feb 1861 | stated five months, x30; **cannot be carried to March** (contractor stopped beef, pp. 361 to 362; "subsistence nearly exhausted" 17 to 19 Apr) | OR I-1 p. 341 |
  | `fb_us_pickens_vogdes_co_a` | 90 | 21 Jan 1861 | stated three months | OR I-1 p. 353; **do not double-count** with the Pickens garrison (ORN I-4 p. 97: the soldiers' supplies were mostly landed) |
  | `fb_us_ship_brooklyn` | 21 | 16 Mar 1861 | stated three weeks, with the exception of flour, butter, sugar, tea and beans | ORN I-4 p. 96 (50 days on 19 Feb, p. 85) |
  | `fb_us_ship_sabine` | 10 | 22 Mar 1861 | computed from "eaten up everything" by 1 to 2 April | ORN I-4 p. 100, estimate |

  Orders, not stocks: *Pawnee* and *Pocahontas* were ordered "one month's stores" (30 March, ORN I-4 pp. 228 to 229). The *Mohawk's* "7 days" and "2 days" are water. Qualitative only: St. Louis (out of bread 22 Mar), *Cumberland*, *Powhatan*, *Macedonian*, *Crusader*, *Supply*, *Wyandotte*.
- **Everything else stays unknown**, about 44 Union blocks (the Army, the Marines, 28 ships), because per-block stores are **not in OR I-1, ORN I-1 or ORN I-4**. Not read: the Secretary of the Navy's reports of 1861, OR I-50 pt 1 (Pacific), OR I-4, ORN II-1. The lead has ruled: no silent default; a **sourced norm per kind of block** as a pack rule with every user flagged "assumed". The norms are Appendix K (N10).
- **Strengths: 23 blocks with unknown strength** (13 Confederate/state, 10 Union incl. ships). The draft CSV carries the helpers' result:
  - **Return:** the St. Louis Arsenal garrison, 488 to 493 (Hagner 21 Feb, Harney 19 Feb: OR I-1 pp. 654 to 655), two weeks before the start. Kershaw's regiment 300 to 500 on 11 April (Whiting; later than the start).
  - **Inferred bands:** Fort Monroe 350 to 450 (seven companies, OR III-1 p. 23, at 59.5 each less 40 detached to Vogdes); New Mexico 1,900 to 2,550 (36 companies, OR III-1 p. 26; the 30 June return is 1,941 present); Pacific 2,650 to 3,150 (about 45 companies, OR III-1 p. 24; 30 June return 2,805 present, 3,361 on the rolls, OR I-50 pt 1 p. 525); Vogdes's company 85 to 100; Fort Morgan 500 to 900 (nine full companies plus 46 men on 4 April); the Louisiana forts about 520; Fort Johnson 60 to 85; Fort Palmetto 50 to 85; the Texas frontier companies 250 to 400; Ford's regiment 600 to 800 (ten companies, early May); Hagood's and Rion's units are later than the start and unsourced.
  - **Snippet-unverified, carrying no figure:** the *Michigan* (88) and *Anacostia* (67) from Wikipedia infoboxes; the Washington Marines (150 to 400; "300 to 400" is from secondary summaries) and Philadelphia Marines (55 to 100).
  - **Nothing found:** the 2nd Artillery Battalion at Charleston, the Galveston batteries.
  - **Cautions:** the 318 infantry recruits at Moultrie (6 March) may be the same men as the "regulars" block; OR III-1 p. 22's 16,367 is the Army's **rolls** total (present and absent); present was 14,663, and OR III-1 p. 700 says 16,006 regulars in April. The only authorized company strength found is 9 Stat. 439 (17 June 1850: up to 74 privates at frontier stations); the 42/64/82 figures come from OR III-5 p. 128 by paraphrase.
- The CSV's `status` column says `proposed`, `unverified-only` or `nothing-found`. Most "proposals" are **inferred bands for lane 1.11 to approve, not returns**.

### The norms for blocks with no stores (the lead's ruling: a sourced norm per kind, flagged "assumed"; Appendix K)

**No regulation sets a stock level**: the *Revised Army Regulations of 1861* (paragraphs 1176 to 1229, pp. 241 to 248) give no months-of-stores rule for posts, and no rule was found in the Navy Regulations (only the 1865 edition was readable). **Every norm below is practice, so every block that uses one must be flagged `assumed`.**

| kind | norm | basis and source | limit |
|---|---|---|---|
| fully stocked garrison post | about 4 months | practice: Sumter on 26 Dec 1860 had 4 months (OR I-1 p. 2); Fort Taylor in January 4 months (OR I-1 p. 343); Pickens with about 100 men on 4 Feb had 6 months (ORN I-4 p. 79) | store each item separately: Sumter's bread ran out first (28 days of bread but 2¼ months of pork on 1 Mar per Hall's inventory in the Lincoln Papers, not the OR; Scott's 12 Mar figures were 26 days of bread and 48 days of pork) |
| a relieving force's load | 3 to 4 months, limited by what the ship could hold | OR I-1 pp. 352 to 353 (the Pickens reinforcement) | an order, not a stock |
| a marching force | 3 days' rations in the haversack, later 2 days; the army at Centreville held 160,000 rations ("5 days' supply") | OR I-2 pp. 308, 318, 337, 343 | one wagon carried about 1,150 to 1,200 rations (pp. 337, 343); a full regiment had 6 wagons per Van Vliet in 1862 (OR I-11 pt 1 p. 157; the 1861 Regulations give no wagon allowance); Patterson's commissary stocks on 9 July were "about ten days" |
| a ship on a short expedition | "ready for sea with one month's stores" | orders: ORN I-4 pp. 228 to 229, 234 | an order; *Brooklyn* on station had 50 days on 19 Feb and 21 days on 16 Mar; coal and water bound steamers at 5 to 8 days before provisions did; the *St. Louis* was fitted for a two-year cruise (p. 202) |
| the ration unit | one ration feeds one man for one day; composition in para 1191 (p. 243) | regulation | the weight (about 2.0 lb net, 2.5 lb with packaging) is the helper's sum from a secondary source: no primary figure found |

**Kinds with no sourceable norm (they stay unknown, visibly):** Fort Monroe, Fort Jefferson, the Texas and Pacific posts; the Commissary General's 1860 or 1861 report was not found; Confederate stocks in 1861 (only the 1862 to 1863 ration cuts); the Navy's "months at sailing" rule.
## Priority 3: the calls and the regiments each state raised

### The Union calls (Appendix F)

- **15 April:** 75,000 militia for three months (the Act of 1795); the Secretary of War's quota table prints **94 regiments and 73,391 men** (OR III-1 p. 69). Pennsylvania's quota was cut from 16 to 14 regiments (p. 77). Three-months men credited: 91,816 per the p. 69 footnote; the p. 699 rows sum to 77,875.
- **3 May:** 42,034 three-year volunteers, 22,714 regulars, 18,000 seamen, "act now, ratify later"; Congress ratified on 6 August. The Acts of 22 and 25 July (500,000 volunteers) are in the Statutes (scan not read).
- **Thin:** three-year counts by state exist only for 21 to 22 June (OR III-1 pp. 285 to 291) and October and December; a 31 July state table was not found. The ninety-day expiry dates rest on mirrors citing Dyer and Bates. Dyer, Phisterer, Fox and Hesseltine's *Lincoln and the War Governors* were not reached. The governors' replies of 15 to 20 April are verified (OR III-1 pp. 70 to 99) and appear under pushback.

### The Confederate calls and the state forces (Appendix G)

- **Acts, confirmed in JCCS:** 28 February (the states' forces and forts), 6 March (Provisional Army, 100,000 volunteers for twelve months), 11 March, 6 May (war and letters of marque), 8 May (additional force for the war, no number).
- **Calls on the governors:** 9 March (places totalling 11,700), 8 April (3,000 per state) and 16 April (5,000): the 8 March "8,000" is not found.
- **Transfers:** North Carolina's convention ordinance of 27 June (OR IV-1 pp. 403 to 404), Arkansas's articles of 15 July (OR I-3 pp. 609 to 610), the Tennessee military league of 7 May (OR IV-1 pp. 296 to 298; the transfer was still under negotiation on 20 and 26 July, OR I-4 pp. 371, 375), Virginia's proclamation of 6 June (OR I-2 p. 912; General Orders 25 on 8 June, p. 911), Lee's command order of 23 April (OR I-2 pp. 775 to 776).
- **By 31 July:** Walker reports "190 regiments and 34 battalions" accepted (OR IV-1 pp. 496 to 498).
- **Thin:** Texas (no numbers; governor not named in the pages read), Tennessee after 2 July, Arkansas and Missouri numbers, the January and February state acts of Georgia, Alabama, Mississippi, Louisiana and Florida. Richardson, the 8 August act text and JCCS after 9 May were not read.

## Priority 4: start loyalty for the border and upper-South states (Appendix H)

Lane 0.3's `control.csv` proposes loyalty only for Delaware, Maryland, Kentucky and Missouri; Virginia, North Carolina, Tennessee and Arkansas rows are empty, and the Virginia and western Virginia proposals (350/300/350 and 650/100/250) sit only in the research note. Verdicts, with the evidence in the appendix:

| state | proposal | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| Delaware | Union 650, Confederate 80 | Union too high and Confederate too low against the 1860 vote |
| Kentucky | Confederate 250 | supported at the low edge; the Union and neutral split has no evidence |
| Missouri | Confederate 200 | at or above the evidence ceiling; it reproduces the 1860 vote arithmetic |
| Maryland | Confederate share inside the band | the band holds; the point values are unsupported |
| Virginia | proposed in the research note | unsupported on 5 March evidence |
| Texas | `confederate:1000` for control | supported, but about a quarter of voters opposed secession |

Counts read: Missouri convention 19 March, 89 to 1 (journal p. 46; the State Archives' 98-1 is wrong); North Carolina 28 February, 47,323 against the convention and 46,672 for (Tribune Almanac 1862 p. 59); Tennessee 9 February, 69,675 against and 57,798 for (a 1900 secondary only). The Almanac's Tennessee and Arkansas 1860 rows were garbled and the helper used the American Presidency Project. Do not invent a number from nothing: the helper gives bands.

## Priority 5: the Confederate opening's decisions (Appendix H)

- **9 April cabinet on Sumter:** no first-hand record found. OR I-1 pp. 297 to 302 hold Walker's 10 and 11 April telegrams and Beauregard's replies, with no minutes, attendees or vote. Tulane dates Davis's decision to 10 April; the 5-1 tally is an inference, not a document.
- **Toombs's "suicide, murder":** earliest print is Stovall, *Robert Toombs* (1892) ch. XXI, p. 226, naming no source; Phillips (1913) p. 235 repeats it. Reagan, Chesnut and Jones in the pages read carry no first-hand account. **It stays unusable.**
- **Walker's order of 10 April** went before the Federal fleet reached the harbour (12 April), against Davis's 29 April framing (Richardson I p. 73). The documents are recorded and the interpretation left to the lead.
- **Upper-South conventions:** each state's dated votes and counts are in Appendix H section 4.1 (the Tennessee, Kentucky and Maryland counts are partly snippet-level); Appendix A has Arkansas's 6 May vote of 69 to 1.
- **The 15 April refusals:** Magoffin OR III-1 p. 70, Ellis p. 72, Jackson pp. 82 to 83 (date line unread); Letcher, Harris and Rector were not found in these pages by this helper (Letcher's is OR III-1 p. 76 in lane 1.9; Harris's p. 81).

| **Seward's "one vote in the Cabinet"** | partial | Frederick Seward, *Seward at Washington* (1891) p. 560, as a narrative remark; the "to his wife" form comes via Sandburg (War Years II p. 300), undated; the primary letter NOT FOUND |

## Fixes after the standing reviewer's read (2026-11-16)

- **S1:** the CSV no longer gives figures for the Washington and Philadelphia Marines (their bands, 150 to 400 and 55 to 100, are kept in the notes column as "not carried"; status `unverified-only`, as for the *Michigan* and *Anacostia*). The 150 days of stores for the Pickens garrison, dated 5 February, is emptied: it does not carry to the 5 March start, no later figure was found, and the status is `unknown-at-start`. Key West (15 January) and Vogdes's company (21 January) carry a note that they are figures for their own dates.
- **S2:** Livermore p. 47's 186,751 is "on the rolls", present and absent, and the 183,588 present figure is dropped (not on his pp. 42 to 47). The Union 31 July bracket is restated as **about 145,000 to 245,000 on the rolls**, with the reason for the lower edge and a warning that a present-for-duty reading would be lower.
- **S3 (the second re-check):** the lower edge of 185,000 was too high: the three-months men's discharges are dated before 31 July in Appendix F, Patterson's dispatches and McDowell's report. The bracket now states both flows, with the arithmetic, and a lower edge of about 145,000; and lane 1.14 is told not to fail a run for falling below it until a primary 31 July return is found.
- **Not confirmed in the reviewer's OCR, re-read in print before entry:** the OR III-1 p. 301 abstract (the reviewer reads about 162,864 aggregate and 108,100 present), the p. 304 "force at command", the p. 22 Army rolls figures, the p. 69 quota table total, and Magoffin's key sentence on p. 70. Unreached by the reviewer: OR IV-1 pp. 496 to 498 and p. 302, OR I-4 p. 15, OR I-3 p. 5, OR I-53 pp. 162 to 163.
- **Page cites:** the *Pawnee* and *Pocahontas* "one month's stores" are ORN I-4 pp. 228 to 229 (not 227); Lincoln's 1 April orders for the *Powhatan* are on pp. 108 to 109. The *Brooklyn* "three weeks" is "with the exception of" the listed items (the earlier "minus" was an unquoted paraphrase).

## Not done, and what is left

Stated plainly so the lead can route it:
- **Hesseltine's *Lincoln and the War Governors*** and Dyer's *Compendium* were not reached (downloads failed), so the Union state-by-state raising to 31 July is thin.
- **Confederate upper bound for men under arms**, Walker's and Cooper's returns (OR IV-1).
- **Per-block stores** for about 44 Union blocks, and the Confederate and state blocks: no source in the volumes read (the norms are Appendix K).
- **Primary tallies** for the Deep South secession votes; the date North Carolina's and Tennessee's admission was completed; the July 1861 Missouri convention journal.
- Sumter's garrison size at the surrender, and Hicks's and the Baltimore papers' accounts of Pratt Street, were not found.
- The reviewer for this lane should re-read the appendices' quotations in print: the fetch tool paraphrased throughout.

## Findings outside my scope

- **Pack data:** `fb_us_ship_powhatan`'s "18 March" arrival date in the repository may be 13 March (the helper's OCR of ORN I-4 p. 265 reads "March 13, 1861"); recheck on a second scan. Key West's letter is OR I-1 p. 343, not 342. The Harriet Lane is not in the force-block CSV.
- **For lane 1.11:** the Confederacy's 21 May Act for the protection of certain Indian tribes; the Pratt Street casualty counts and the Sumter terms are inputs to the registered gate topics (`civilian-deaths-1861`, `prisoners-and-exchange`) and should be read against lane 1.8's register before entry.
- **For lane 1.14:** the Union men-under-arms bracket (about 145,000 to 245,000 on the rolls) is inferred; if the checkpoint wants a tighter band, a primary 31 July return is needed.
- **For the steward:** the OPEN_QUESTIONS line "about forty blocks" should be corrected (it was the list of blocks with no stores found), and the quota and act-date corrections above filed.

# Appendices: the evidence, as the research helpers recorded it

Each appendix is one helper's file, unedited except for a title line. Quality tags and page-number caveats are the helpers' own.


## Appendix A: States, sides, Washington (helper N1)


Quality keys: `return` = dated official or primary text I read in this run; `estimate` = a secondary or compiled source I read that states it; `inferred` = my reading or calendar arithmetic; `snippet-unverified` = a search summary or fetch-tool paraphrase only (never used to carry a value). "AFTER WINDOW" = later than 31 July 1861. Page numbers come from OCR running heads and can drift by a page.

### 1. Which states had seceded by 31 July 1861

#### 1a. Admission to the Confederacy (primary: CSAL, JCCS)

| State | Entry | Date | Source | Quality |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SC, GA, FL, AL, MS, LA | The six states whose deputies framed and signed the Provisional Constitution at Montgomery; the government dates from **8 Feb 1861** | 8 Feb 1861 | CSAL title page ("from the institution of the government, February 8, 1861") and Provisional Constitution signature lists | return |
| Texas | "An Act to admit Texas as a Member of the Confederate States of America", ch. XXIV, passed in secret session and approved **2 Mar 1861**; the same day Congress authorised the Texas deputies to sign the Provisional Constitution | 2 Mar 1861 | CSAL ch. XXIV; JCCS Sat. 2 Mar 1861 (bill read three times and passed; President's message that he had signed it) | return |
| Virginia | "An Act to admit the Commonwealth of Virginia as a member of the Confederate States", ch. IV, reciting that Virginia "in a convention of her people, ratified and adopted" the Provisional Constitution. Bill passed 7 May (JCCS p.~192-193); the President's message on 8 May says he "yesterday approved and signed" it | **7 May 1861** | CSAL ch. IV, "Approved May 7, 1861" (p. ~101); JCCS 7 and 8 May | return |
| North Carolina | "An Act to admit the State of North Carolina into the Confederacy, on a certain condition", ch. XXV: admission on condition that the NC convention adopt and ratify the Provisional Constitution and transmit it; on receipt "the President, by proclamation, shall announce the fact; whereupon ... the admission ... shall be considered as complete". Passed 16 May (JCCS p.235, 8 states yea) | act **17 May 1861**; completion date **not found** | CSAL ch. XXV p.118 ("Approved May 17, 1861"); JCCS 16-17 May | return (act); completion needs-source |
| Tennessee | "An Act to admit the State of Tennessee into the Confederacy, on a certain condition", ch. XXVI, same form: condition that the "properly and legally constituted authorities" adopt the Provisional Constitution and the Governor send the proceedings; then presidential proclamation completes admission. Passed 16 May (JCCS p.235) | act **17 May 1861**; completion date **not found** | CSAL ch. XXVI p.119; JCCS 16-17 May | return (act); completion needs-source |
| Arkansas | "An Act to admit the State of Arkansas into the Confederacy", ch. XXX, outright: recites the convention's "ordinance dissolving their political connection with the United States" and "another ordinance adopting and ratifying" the Provisional Constitution. Bill passed Sat. 18 May (8 states yea, JCCS p.244); President's message of 20 May that he had signed it (JCCS p.256) | passed 18 May, **approved 20 May 1861** | CSAL ch. XXX p.120 ("Approved May 20, 1861"); JCCS | return |

**The Tennessee and Confederate military league:** the convention "between the State of Tennessee and the Confederate States", signed at Nashville **7 May 1861**, put Tennessee's forces under the President's control "until the said State shall become a member"; President Davis sent it to Congress with a message dated 13 May (read in executive session 15 May, JCCS pp.223-224) (return).

**Not established:** the date on which the President's proclamation completed the admission of North Carolina and Tennessee. A secondary list (Wikipedia, via a search summary) gives Tennessee as 2 July 1861: `snippet-unverified`, not to be used as a value. JCCS's later pages (Provisional Congress, 20 July onward, "second session at Richmond") name both states' delegates but I did not find a proclamation text.

| Kentucky | Statute V, ch. V, "An Act for the admission of the State of Kentucky into the Confederate States of America, as a member thereof", "Approved December 10, 1861"; ARCH: passed 9 Dec | **10 Dec 1861** (AFTER WINDOW) | CSAL Statute V; ARCH Kentucky note (JCCS 1:546-547) | return (act); ARCH secondary for the 9 Dec passage and the Russellville convention (18-20 Nov 1861, George W. #### 1c. Secession ordinance, popular vote, and Confederate entry, by state

| State | Ordinance (convention or legislature) | Popular ratification | Confederate entry | Source, quality |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| South Carolina | **20 Dec 1860**, passed "by the unanimous vote of the convention", which had assembled 17 Dec | none | one of the six (8 Feb 1861); convention re-assembled 26 Mar 1861 and adopted the Confederate constitution, 140 ayes, 29 noes (AAC p.657, which calls it "Provisional") | AAC (read); CSAL. estimate |
| Mississippi | **9 Jan 1861**, ayes 84, noes 15 | none (a motion to submit the Constitution to voters failed 32-53: fetch paraphrase, `snippet-unverified`) | one of the six, 8 Feb 1861 (CSAL) | AAC (read). estimate |
| Florida | **10 Jan 1861**, 62 ayes, 7 noes (7 Jan resolution on the right and duty to secede: 62-5) | none stated | one of the six, 8 Feb 1861 | AAC (read). estimate |
| Alabama | **11 Jan 1861**, secret session. AAC prints "ayes, 61; noes, 89": a misprint on its face (100 delegates; 61-39 is the usual figure, **not verified in a primary**) | none: proposals to submit it to the people were laid on the table, 54-33 (AAC); convention ratified the Confederate Constitution **13 Mar 1861**, 87-5 | one of the six, 8 Feb 1861 | AAC (read). estimate; vote needs-source |
| Georgia | **19 Jan 1861**, ayes 208, noes 89 (the 18 Jan resolution to secede: 165-130; AAC) | none (a substitute and a delay to 3 March were lost). D. 1861" (AAC prints the text; sec. 2 submits it to the people on 23 Feb and, unless rejected, it takes effect 2 Mar). AAC gives a roll of "ayes 106, nays 7" on "the 5th"; the TSHA page (fetch paraphrase) gives 166-8 on 1 Feb | **23 Feb 1861**: AAC gives 34,794 for, 11,285 against "in eighty counties" (partial); TSHA (fetch paraphrase) gives 44,317-13,020; the figure often cited, 46,153-14,747, was not read. The convention counted the vote 2-4 Mar and declared Texas independent (AAC) | admitted by act **2 Mar 1861** (CSAL, JCCS); convention ordinance of 5 Mar gave Texas's adhesion to the Provisional Constitution (AAC) | AAC read; CSAL/JCCS return. Votes: conflict, see Conflicts |
| Virginia | **17 Apr 1861**, in secret session, "to take effect ... should the same be ratified by the people" on the fourth Thursday of May; ayes 88, nays 55 (AAC: "rumored"; the convention did not publish the vote) | **23 May 1861**: Governor Letcher's proclamation: for ratification 125,950, for rejection 20,873, majority 105,577; returns from 34 northwestern counties not received (AAC p.~739, prints the proclamation). WVA: "May 23 The Ordinance of Secession was approved by the voters of Virginia." NPS-CDW says the referendum was "three to one" for secession | Convention with the Confederate States, Stephens commissioner, **24 Apr 1861**, approved and ratified by the Virginia convention **25 Apr** (AAC p.737); admitted by act **7 May**; the Virginia convention passed an ordinance adopting the Confederate Constitution **19 Jun 1861** (WVA June timeline) | AAC, WVA, NPS-CDW read; CSAL/JCCS return |
| Arkansas | **6 May 1861**, 69 ayes, 1 no (AAC); the convention had rejected a conditional ordinance referring the question to the people, 35-39, in March | none | passed 18 May, approved 20 May (CSAL ch. XXX); AAC: "On the 18th of May, Arkansas was admitted ... her delegates took their seats" | AAC read; CSAL/JCCS return |
| North Carolina | Convention assembled **20 May 1861** (the anniversary of the Mecklenburg Declaration); the ordinance of secession is "Done at Raleigh, 20th day of May" (AAC prints it; AAC's narrative says the 21st: conflict). A second ordinance of the same date ratified the Provisional Constitution, to join "when admitted by the Congress" | none: the convention acted alone | conditional act 17 May (CSAL); completion by proclamation, date not found | AAC read; CSAL/JCCS return |
| Tennessee | No convention. The General Assembly on **6 May 1861** passed "An Act to submit to a vote of the people a Declaration of Independence" and ordinance dissolving the federal relations (Senate 20-4, House 46-21; AAC), and ratified the military league of 7 May | **8 Jun 1861**: for "Separation" 104,913, against 47,238, majority 57,675 (AAC table: East Tennessee 14,780-32,923; Middle 58,265-8,198; West 29,127-6,117; military camps 2,741 for). The Act required returns by 24 Jun and the Governor's proclamation. The same act submitted adoption of the Provisional Constitution | conditional act 17 May (CSAL); completion by proclamation, date not found | AAC read; CSAL/JCCS return |

**Eleven by ordinance or declaration; not all eleven were full members on 31 July in the statutes' own terms.** Texas, Virginia and Arkansas were admitted outright by act (2 Mar, 7 May, 20 May). North Carolina and Tennessee were admitted "on a certain condition" on 17 May, complete on a presidential proclamation whose date I did not find; both were bound by convention or league from 7 May (Tennessee) and 20 May (NC). Popular ratification happened in three states only: Texas (23 Feb), Virginia (23 May), Tennessee (8 Jun).

#### 1d. Missouri, Kentucky, Maryland and Delaware had NOT seceded on 31 July 1861

| State | Statement | Dated acts | Source, quality |
|---|---|---|---|
| Missouri | No ordinance of secession was in force on 31 July 1861. The only convention elected to decide the question voted against secession; the "secession" act of the Jackson legislature at Neosho is **28 Oct 1861** (AFTER WINDOW) | 16 Jan 1861 General Assembly act for a convention, "Not a single advocate of secession was elected"; **28 Feb 1861** convention opened; **19 Mar 1861** voted against secession, **98-1**; **22 Mar** the convention adjourned "until the third Monday in December", Sterling Price presiding (MoJ, Friday 22 Mar entry); the 31 July convention is in section 2 | MoSA timeline (98-1, read); MoJ (adjournment, read; the 98-1 count is **not** found in the Journal's OCR text); ARCH |
| Kentucky | No secession in force. The legislature (Union majority) and Governor Magoffin declared neutrality; the Confederate "state government" at Russellville exists only from 18-20 Nov 1861 (AFTER WINDOW) | 25 Jan 1861 General Assembly called a border-state convention for Frankfort on 27 May; **16 May** House neutrality resolution, **24 May** Senate concurrence; **20 May** Magoffin's proclamation (section 2) | ARCH (read, secondary); UNI ScholarWorks record of the proclamation text (`scholarworks.uni.edu/nhomefront/6`, catalogue page read, text not read); AAC prints the proclamation |
| Maryland | No ordinance. The General Assembly met at Frederick from 26 Apr 1861; by 29 Apr it had declared it had no power to pass an ordinance of secession (Maryland Historical Magazine 2006 via a search summary: `snippet-unverified`); AAC prints the report of the committee on Federal Relations (a memorial of 216 Prince George's voters asking for an ordinance), the minority report to substitute the memorial's prayer being rejected **13 ayes, 53 noes** (the date is not printed in AAC; 53-12 appears in another snippet) | Hicks's message of 25/26 Apr: "The only safety of Maryland lies in preserving a neutral position" (MSA exhibit quotes 25 Apr; AAC prints it in the 26 Apr message) | AAC p.446 read; MSA exhibit read |
| Delaware | No ordinance. The legislature (assembled at Dover 2 Jan 1861) heard Mississippi's commissioner Henry Dickinson on 3 Jan and resolved "unanimously" in the House (Senate concurring "by a majority") its "unqualified disapproval" of the remedy proposed in Mississippi's resolutions | 2 Jan assembly; 3 Jan Dickinson's address and reply (reply date not printed); 23 Apr Governor Burton's proclamation recommending volunteer companies; 1 May orders to muster at Washington | AAC pp.256-257 read (secondary); primary House Journal not read |

### 2. The side of each government that could change by 31 July

Lead sentence for the lead: **on 31 July 1861 Maryland, Delaware and (provisional) Missouri were governed by Union-side governments; Kentucky's government was neutral; the Restored Government of Virginia at Wheeling was Union; the Indian nations were divided, with the Creek, Choctaw and Chickasaw bound to the Confederacy by treaty and the Cherokee, Seminole and the small peoples not yet.**

#### 2a. Missouri (Union by 31 July; two rival governments from that day)

| Date | Act | Source, quality |
|---|---|---|
| 28 Feb 1861 | State convention opens at Jefferson City | MoSA (read), MoJ (read) |
| 19 Mar | Convention votes 98-1 against secession | MoSA (read) |
| 22 Mar | Convention adjourns to the third Monday in December, Price president (MoJ) | MoJ (read) |
| 20-21 Apr | Liberty Arsenal seized by armed men; Nathaniel Grant's report dated Sunday 21 Apr says it was taken "yesterday about 10 o'clock" | OR I-1 p.649 (read). return |
| 10 May | Camp Jackson Affair: Lyon captures 669 state militia; at least 28 civilians killed; Gov. Claiborne F. Jackson's attempts to tip the state to the Confederacy culminate | MoSA (read) |
| 11 Jun | Planters' House conference, Lyon with Jackson and Price (a secondary summary; date and "This means war" account from the Ozarks "Community and Conflict" page and the Boman biography it cites) | ozarkscivilwar.org (read), secondary |
| 13 or 15 Jun | Jackson abandons Jefferson City before Lyon occupies it: **13 Jun** (MoSA) against **15 Jun** (ARCH citing OR I-3 pp.12-14; the Ozarks page says 15 June). Conflict, not resolved | MoSA, ARCH read |
| 30 Jul | State convention passes an ordinance declaring the offices of governor, lieutenant governor, secretary of state and the General Assembly vacant (ARCH: **30 Jul**; MoSA: "vacant on **July 31**") | ARCH, MoSA (read). estimate |
| **31 Jul** | Convention elects **Hamilton R. Gamble** provisional governor (ARCH: elected **31 Jul**; MoSA: "appointed provisional Governor"; other sources 1 Aug; the Office of Administration "Hall of Governors" page says only "mid-July": conflict). New elections were to follow in November | ARCH, MoSA, `oa.mo.gov/hall_of_governors/Gamble` read |
| 5 Aug (AFTER WINDOW) | Jackson proclaims Missouri "a free republic", dissolving ties with the Union | MoSA |
| 10 Aug (AFTER WINDOW) | Wilson's Creek | MoSA |
| 20 Aug, 28 Oct, 28 Nov (AFTER WINDOW) | Confederate conditional admission act; Neosho act of secession (passed 28 Oct, signed 31 Oct per ARCH); admission 28 Nov | CSAL; MoSA; ARCH |

Who held the government on 31 July: the **convention's provisional government under Gamble (Union)**, installed that day; the elected governor Jackson held a rival claim in the field and from the Confederacy's side. That Lincoln's administration recognised Gamble is stated in a search summary only: `snippet-unverified`.

#### 2b. Kentucky (neutral; government Governor Beriah Magoffin; legislature with a Union majority)

| Date | Act | Source, quality |
|---|---|---|
| 25 Jan 1861 | General Assembly calls for a border-state convention at Frankfort, 27 May | ARCH (read) |
| 15 Apr | Magoffin telegraphs Secretary Cameron: Kentucky "will furnish no troops for the wicked purpose of subduing her sister Southern States" | AAC p.396 (prints the telegram). estimate |
| 3-6 May | Governor orders an election for the extra session of Congress and calls the legislature into extra session (AAC: for 6 May; an earlier mention of 27 Apr is also printed) | AAC p.396 |
| 4 May | Election of delegates to the Border State Convention; Union vote 98,501 of 146,216 (the November 1860 aggregate) | AAC p.397 |
| 16 May | House of Representatives passes the neutrality resolution (ARCH: 16 May; AAC: "On the 17th") | ARCH, AAC |
| **20 May** | **Magoffin's proclamation** forbidding any movement upon Kentucky soil by "the United and Confederate States"; AAC prints the text; its preamble, per a search summary, cites requests that he forbid the march of forces on Cairo | UNI ScholarWorks catalogue ("Proclamation by the Governor of Kentucky, May 20, 1861"); AAC p.397 |
| 22 May | House rejects a resolution that the proclamation stated Kentucky's position (AAC) | AAC |
| 24 May | Senate concurs in neutrality (ARCH) | ARCH |
| 27 May | Border State Convention at Frankfort: only Kentucky and Missouri represented (AAC) | AAC |
| **20 Jun** | Special election for the extra session of Congress: Union men chosen in all districts but the first, where H. C. Burnett (States Rights) won (AAC prints "30th of June"; 30 June 1861 was a Sunday, so 20 June is `inferred`; the brief's date fits) | AAC; calendar |
| After window | 7 Aug Confederate act authorising enlistments in Kentucky; the August elections and the 2 Sep legislature (AAC); Russellville convention 18-20 Nov; CS admission 10 Dec | AAC; ARCH; CSAL |

**Side on 31 July:** neutral by proclamation, with a Union-majority legislature and a southern-sympathising governor; the August elections that gave the Unionists their legislature are after the window.

#### 2c. Maryland (Union side; Governor Thomas H. Hicks; secession-leaning legislature kept in recess)

| Date | Act | Source, quality |
|---|---|---|

| 19 Apr | Baltimore riot: 6th Massachusetts attacked on Pratt Street; five soldiers and twelve civilians killed (MSA exhibit); that night Hicks, Mayor George W. Brown and Marshal Kane agreed to burn the railroad bridges north of Baltimore and cut telegraph lines, isolating the city from the north (MSA case 4); Hicks later said he left the matter to the Mayor | MSA (read) |

| 13 May | Butler takes Federal Hill, Baltimore: he marched from the Relay House "at 6 o'clock" with 500 of the 6th Massachusetts, 450 of the 8th New York and a section of Cook's battery on Monday evening; the OR's editor writes "Monday [12th instant]", but 13 May 1861 was a Monday (4 Mar 1861 was a Monday) | OR I-2 p.29 (read). return; date by calendar `inferred` |
| 14 May | Both houses adjourn to 4 Jun at Frederick (AAC) | AAC |
| 13 Jun | Congressional election day set by the Governor; Hicks's December message says the votes of 13 Jun and 6 Nov showed Maryland's loyalty | AAC p.446-448 |
| 4 Jun | House meets at Frederick (Archives of Maryland vol. 430 p.161) | MSA (read). return |
| 30 Jul-7 Aug | Special session reconvenes (MSA: 30 Jul to 7 Aug; AAC: assembled 31 Jul and adjourned to 17 Sep) | MSA; AAC. conflict of a day |
| 17 Sep (AFTER WINDOW) | Planned reassembly prevented by arrests of secession members | AAC |

**Side:** Union. Federal troops held Baltimore from 13 May and Annapolis from late April; the governor was Union; the legislature's secession-leaning majority was dispersed by recess and (after the window) arrests.

| **11-25 Jun** | **Second Wheeling Convention** assembles at Washington Hall 11 Jun; Arthur Boreman president 12 Jun; Carlile's "Declaration of the People of Virginia" 13 Jun; Declaration of Rights 17 Jun; **19 Jun** votes the ordinance reorganising the government of Virginia; **20 Jun** elects **Francis H. Pierpont** governor (unanimously), who takes the oath that day; adjourns 25 Jun until August | WVA June timeline (read). return |
| 1-26 Jul | Pierpont calls the General Assembly of the Reorganized (Restored) Government into extra session (1 Jul); Daniel Frost speaker, Daniel Polsley lieutenant governor presiding over the Senate (2 Jul); Carlile, W. G. Brown and K. V. Whaley take their House seats in Washington (4 Jul); 9 Jul it elects officers and Carlile and Waitman T. Willey US Senators; Willey and Carlile "formally seated" by the Senate 13 Jul; extra session ends 26 Jul; 23 Jul the House of Delegates debates a bill for a new state | WVA July timeline (read). return |
| 11-13 Jul | Rich Mountain (11 Jul), Laurel Hill surrender (12 Jul), Corrick's Ford (13 Jul) | WVA |
| 25 Jul | Rosecrans receives the Department of the Ohio, including much of western Virginia | WVA |

The statehood vote (August and later) is AFTER WINDOW. Lincoln's own recognition of the Pierpont government is shown by the Senate seating of its senators (13 Jul): that is what WVA states; a presidential letter was not read.

#### 2f. The Indian Territory nations (the repository note checked against primary pages)

What I read: **CSAL** (the treaties, "Indian Treaties", pp.289-384), **OR I-1 pp.648-650**, **OR I-13 p.490** (Ross, 17 May). The repository note's claims about the Creek chiefs, ratification, places and parties **check out against CSAL**; its fort dates **check out against OR I-1 p.648 and p.650** except as noted.

**The Act that opened the treaties:** every 1861 treaty recites "An act for the protection of certain Indian tribes", **approved 21 May 1861**, offering the protectorate of the nations "west of Arkansas and Missouri". Pike is "Commissioner, with plenary powers". (CSAL preambles.) The 13 March 1861 date for Pike's commission is **not verified** (not found in the pages read; needs-source).

| Nation or people | Act | Date | Where made / who signed | Source, quality | By 31 Jul? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Creek (Muscogee)** | "Treaty of friendship and alliance" with the Creek Nation "by its Chiefs, Head Men and Warriors in General Council assembled", offensive and defensive alliance, Creek nation under Confederate protection (Arts. I-II). Signed by Motey Kinnaird (Principal Chief), Icho Hacho (Principal Chief Upper Creeks) and 15 others. **Ratified by the Creek General Council "on this, the twentieth day of July" at the Council Ground** | made **10 Jul 1861**, ratified **20 Jul** | North Fork Village, on the North Fork of the Canadian, in the Creek Nation | CSAL p.289-310 (read). return | **Yes** |
| **Choctaw** | Treaty of friendship and alliance with the Choctaw (commissioners "appointed by the Principal Chief ... in pursuance of an act of the Legislature") and Chickasaw Nations (commissioners "elected by the Legislature") | **12 Jul 1861** | North Fork Village, Creek Nation | CSAL p.311-331 (read). return | **Yes** |
| **Chickasaw** | same treaty, 16 Chickasaw commissioners (OCR-garbled list) including Edmund Pickens and Holmes Colbert | **12 Jul 1861** | North Fork Village | CSAL (read). return. The Chickasaw legislature's act of 25 May (REPO-IT, citing the nation's own pages) is **not re-read** | **Yes** |
| **Seminole** | "Treaty of friendship", made "at the Seminole Council House in the Seminole Nation" by "Chiefs, head men and warriors, in General Council assembled" | **1 Aug 1861** (AFTER WINDOW) | Seminole Council House | CSAL p.332-346 (read). return | **No** (by one day) |
| **Cherokee** | Ross's proclamation of neutrality, Park Hill, "strict neutrality between the States threatening civil war"; "observance of unswerving neutrality between them" | **17 May 1861** | Executive office, Park Hill | OR I-13 p.490 (read). return | neutral on 31 Jul |
| Cherokee treaty | Treaty with the Cherokee Nation concluded at Tahlequah (index p.~384) | **7 Oct 1861** (AFTER WINDOW); REPO-IT adds that a General Convention of the Cherokee People at Tahlequah on 21 Aug authorised it | Tahlequah | CSAL index and text (read); the 21 Aug convention is from the treaty's text per REPO-IT, not re-read | **No** |
| **Osage** | Treaty with the Great Osage tribe | **2 Oct 1861** (AFTER WINDOW) | Park Hill | CSAL index and place line (read) | No |
| **Quapaw** | Treaty with the Quapaw tribe | **4 Oct 1861** (AFTER WINDOW) | Park Hill | CSAL | No |
| Seneca; Senecas and Shawnees | Treaty with the Seneca (of Sandusky) and the Shawnees of the mixed bands | **4 Oct 1861** (AFTER WINDOW) | Park Hill | CSAL | No |
| Wichita and affiliated peoples (leased district) | Treaty with the Pen-e-tegh-ca Band of the Ne-um or Comanches and the tribes and bands of Wichitas, Cado-Ha-da-chos, Hae-cos, Ta-hua-ca-ros, A-na-dagh-cos, Ton-ca-wes, Ai-o-nais, Ki-chais, Shawnees and Delawares (index spellings); a second treaty with the Comanches of the Prairies and Staked Plain | **12 Aug 1861** (AFTER WINDOW) | Wichita Agency | CSAL index (read) | No |

**Which nations were allied to the Confederacy by treaty by 31 July:** Creek (10 Jul, ratified 20 Jul), Choctaw and Chickasaw (12 Jul). Not by 31 July: Seminole (1 Aug), Cherokee (7 Oct; neutral since 17 May), Osage, Quapaw, Seneca-Shawnee (Oct), Wichita and affiliated peoples (12 Aug). **The Creek treaty was not unanimous**: Icho Hacho signed; Opothleyahola, leader of the "loyal" Upper Creeks, did not (REPO-IT, citing Bahos and the Muscogee Nation's study guide; I did not re-read those; the signer list in CSAL confirms Opothleyahola is not among the 17 names, though the OCR is garbled).

**The Union posts abandoned (OR I-1 pp.648-650, read; primary):**

| Post | Event | Source |
|---|---|---|
| **Fort Smith** (Arkansas, outside the nations) | Sturgis: "evacuated that post at 9 o'clock p.m. on the 23rd ultimo" (23 Apr 1861); Maj. Gatlin on 24 Apr reports Arkansas state troops entered; Sturgis reached Fort Washita 30 Apr | OR I-1 p.650 |
| **Fort Washita** | Emory (report of 19 May): "The day after I left Washita [April 16] the Texas occupied that place in force". The bracketed date is the OR editor's; Emory had received Scott's orders of 17 Apr two days' march from Washita; so Emory left about 15 or 16 Apr (REPO-IT's "left about 15-16 April") | OR I-1 p.648 |
| **Fort Arbuckle** | "The troops at Arbuckel and two companies from Cobb joined me five miles from Arbuckle ... May 3"; on the 5th "Arbuckel was occupied by a large force of white people from Texas" | OR I-1 p.648 |
| **Fort Cobb** | Emory on 9 May found the Cobb command (two companies of foot) thirty-five miles northeast of the post; no date of occupation printed | OR I-1 p.648 |
| Emory's column | 11 companies, 750 men and 150 non-combatants reached Kansas; Emory at Fort Leavenworth 31 May | OR I-1 pp.648-649 |

Emory also writes of "the unworthy conduct of the governor of the Chickasaw Nation" who "busily joined in an attempt to disarm and disgrace the soldiers" (OR I-1 p.648; governor not named). Not read: OR I-1 pp.651-662 (Benning, Campbell, Young, Townsend); REPO-IT relies on them.

---

### 3. Was Washington held on 31 July 1861? **Yes.**

On 31 July 1861 the Union held Washington, the District of Columbia, Alexandria, Arlington Heights, and the Virginia end of the Potomac bridges, with the Army of Northeastern Virginia (McDowell) back inside the defenses after Bull Run and McClellan assuming command around Washington at the end of July (NPS-CDW: "McClellan assumed command of the troops around Washington, D.C. at the end of that month"). No Confederate force held any part of the District.

| Date | Event | Source, quality |
|---|---|---|
| 14 Apr | Sumter surrenders | NPS-CDW |
| 18 Apr | Pennsylvania militia and Minnesota regulars reach Washington, joining about 600 District militia, 200 Marines and about 600 regulars | NPS-CDW (read), estimate |
| **19 Apr** | Baltimore riot (Pratt Street); the 6th Massachusetts reaches Washington (NPS-CDW "the next day" after 18 Apr); Baltimore's bridges burned and telegraph wires cut that night: **Washington isolated from the North** | MSA cases 2 and 4; NPS-CDW |
| 21 Apr | Word reaches Washington that the 1st Rhode Island, 7th New York and Butler's Massachusetts troops are offshore at Annapolis; land and rebuild the railroad | NPS-CDW |
| 25 Apr | 7th New York arrives in Washington; 1st Rhode Island begins arriving 26 Apr; "within five days" 7,500 volunteers quartered in the city, nearly 11,000 by the end of April (NPS-CDW) | NPS-CDW. estimate |
| 27-28 Apr | Mansfield takes over the Department of Washington; Barnard attached as chief engineer 28 Apr | NPS-CDW |
| 13 May | Butler's occupation of Baltimore (Federal Hill) ends the Baltimore threat; Annapolis-Washington railroad open, "5,000 troops daily" (Butler, 15 May) | OR I-2 pp.29-30 |
| 23 May | Virginia's referendum | WVA, NPS-CDW |
| **24 May** | Union troops cross the Potomac at 2 a.m. by the Long Bridge and the Aqueduct Bridge and by steamer to Alexandria, occupying **Arlington Heights** and **Alexandria**; Ellsworth (11th NY, Fire Zouaves) is killed at Alexandria; a Confederate troop of Virginia cavalry captured. Heintzelman's list: the 69th, 5th, 28th NY by the Aqueduct; 12th, 25th, 7th NY and 3rd New Jersey (Runyon) by the Long Bridge; 1st Michigan; 1st New York Zouaves by steamer | OR I-2 pp.38-40 (Sandford's report; Heintzelman's report of 20 Jul 1863; read). return |
| 25-27 May | Entrenchments at Arlington; 8th NY on Arlington Heights 26 May; 1st New Jersey in the Long Bridge trenches 27 May | OR I-2 p.39 (Sandford) |
| 27 May | War Dept General Orders No. 26 creates the Department of Northeastern Virginia under McDowell (NPS-CDW cites OR I-2 pp.653-654); McDowell assumes command 28 May (General Orders No. 1) | NPS-CDW (read); order text not read |
| 31 May-1 Jun | First Confederate probes: skirmish at Fairfax Court House (Tompkins; Capt. John Q. Marr killed) | spirit61.info (secondary; OR I-2 report not read) |
| 17 Jun | Vienna: Schenck's train ambushed by Confederates under Maxcy Gregg; nine Union dead (spirit61.info, secondary); the Union line then stalled "from Alexandria to Chain Bridge and Little Falls" | spirit61.info, secondary |
| 18-21 Jul | Blackburn's Ford (18 Jul, Richardson, casualties 1 officer and 18 men killed, 1 officer and 37 wounded, 83 missing: OR I-2 p.314); **Bull Run, 21 Jul**: McDowell at 5:45 p.m. reports "driven from the position we had gained"; "a confused mob, entirely demoralized"; no stand possible "this side of the Potomac" | OR I-2 pp.314-316 (read). return |
| 22-23 Jul | The army returns through Fairfax to the Potomac; **no Confederate attack on Washington followed** | OR I-2 p.316 |

**Forces present** (OR III-1 p.301, "Consolidated abstract from returns of the U. S. Army on or about June 30, 1861", read; return):

| Command | Officers present for duty | Men present for duty | Aggregate present | Aggregate present and absent | Return date |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Department of Washington (Mansfield) | 1,693 | 36,608 | 41,160 | 43,363 | 30 Jun 1861 |
| Department of Northeastern Virginia (McDowell) | 764 | 13,666 | 16,611 | 16,611 | 26 Jun 1861 |
| Department of Annapolis (Banks) | none filed | | | | no return |
| Department of Pennsylvania (Patterson) | 747 | 15,865 | 16,694 | 17,188 | 30 Jun 1861 |
| Department of Virginia (Butler) | 397 | 7,794 | 9,485 | 9,929 | 30 Jun 1861 |

(The abstract's note warns that it omits unattached officers, recruits in rendezvous and organisations in process of formation. The OR page's table layout is jumbled by the transcription; the row pairing matches the totals shown. There is no return for 31 July itself.) McDowell's army organisation for 21 July (five divisions) is OR I-2 pp.314-315 (read): First Division Tyler, Second Hunter, Third Heintzelman, Fourth (Reserve) Runyon, Fifth Miles, with 29 or more named regiments; strengths were not read.

### 4. Checkpoint band: items | expected status on 31 July 1861 | sources

| Item | Expected status on 31 Jul 1861 | Sources |
|---|---|---|
| SC, MS, FL, AL, GA, LA | seceded Dec 1860-Jan 1861; original six of the Confederacy (8 Feb) | AAC, CSAL |
| Texas | seceded (1 Feb; popular vote 23 Feb; effect 2 Mar); admitted 2 Mar | CSAL, JCCS, AAC |
| Virginia | seceded 17 Apr; ratified 23 May (125,950-20,873 as proclaimed, western returns missing); admitted 7 May | AAC, CSAL |
| Arkansas | seceded 6 May (69-1); admitted 20 May | AAC, CSAL, JCCS |
| North Carolina | seceded 20 May; admitted "on a condition" 17 May; completion date unknown | AAC, CSAL |
| Tennessee | declaration 6 May; popular vote 8 Jun, 104,913-47,238; admitted "on a condition" 17 May; completion date unknown | AAC, CSAL |
| Missouri | **not seceded**; Union provisional government (Gamble) from 31 Jul; Confederate admission 20 Aug / 28 Nov = AFTER WINDOW | MoSA, ARCH, CSAL |
| Kentucky | **not seceded**; neutral by proclamation 20 May; Union-majority legislature; Confederate admission 10 Dec = AFTER WINDOW | ARCH, AAC, CSAL |
| Maryland | **not seceded**; Union; Hicks governor; Federal troops at Baltimore since 13 May; legislature declared no power to secede (29 Apr) | MSA, AAC, OR I-2 p.29 |
| Delaware | **not seceded**; Union; Burton governor; legislature rejected Mississippi's overture 3 Jan | AAC |
| Western Virginia | Union; Restored Government at Wheeling, Pierpont governor since 20 Jun; senators seated | WVA |
| Creek, Choctaw, Chickasaw | allied to the Confederacy by treaty (10 and 12 Jul) | CSAL |
| Cherokee | neutral (Ross, 17 May); treaty 7 Oct = AFTER WINDOW | OR I-13 p.490, CSAL |
| Seminole | not yet; treaty 1 Aug = AFTER WINDOW | CSAL |
| Osage, Quapaw, Seneca-Shawnee, Wichita peoples | not yet; treaties Aug-Oct = AFTER WINDOW | CSAL |
| Union posts in the Indian Territory | Fort Smith evacuated 23 Apr; Washita left about 15-16 Apr; Arbuckle's troops left 3 May and Texans in 5 May; Cobb's command gone before 9 May | OR I-1 pp.648-650 |
| Washington | **held**; isolated 19-25 Apr; Arlington Heights and Alexandria occupied 24 May; Bull Run lost 21 Jul but the capital not attacked; Dept of Washington about 41,000 present (30 Jun) | OR I-2, OR III-1 p.301, NPS-CDW |

### Gaps (needs-source)

- Date of the President's proclamation completing the admission of **North Carolina and Tennessee** (CSAL ch. XXV and XXVI make it the completing act). Look: JCCS 2nd session pages from 20 Jul 1861 (the OCR text is at `archive.org/stream/journalofcongres00conf/journalofcongres00conf_djvu.txt`, lines ~24500 onward), the Richardson *Messages and Papers of the Confederacy*, or the Confederate State Department's proclamations.
- **Primary texts of the secession votes** (convention journals) for AL (61-39?), TX (166-8 and the 23 Feb county returns), SC, MS, FL, GA, LA, AR: I used AAC (secondary). URLs that failed or were not tried: `tn.gov/tsla/exhibits/civilwar/secession.htm` (403), `floridamemory.com/items/show/204` (403), `encyclopediaofalabama.org/article/h-1488` (wrong page), `ncpedia.org/history/cw-1900/secession-convention` (403), Encyclopedia Virginia ordinance entry (404), HathiTrust (blocked by rule).
- **Missouri convention July 1861 journal** (*Journal of the Missouri State Convention, held at Jefferson City, July 1861*) for the 30-31 July ordinance and election: catalogued, not found in text. The March volume's count of 98-1 not found in its OCR.
- **Kentucky**: House Journal for 16 May; the text of the 20 May proclamation (only AAC's reprint read); the 20 June returns from a primary.
- **Maryland**: the House journal entry for the vote on secession of 29 April (Archives of Maryland vol. 430, earlier pages); Hicks's call of the legislature (proclamation of 22 April); *Archives of Maryland* pages near the Butler arrival.
- **Delaware**: the legislature's January 1861 resolution text and date (House and Senate journals).
- **Pike's commission, 13 March 1861**; **Choctaw and Chickasaw legislatures' ratifications**; **Chickasaw 25 May act**; OR I-1 pp.651-662 and p.667.
- **Union strength on 31 July itself** (no return exists in the sources read; the 30 June abstract is the nearest). McDowell's Bull Run strengths by regiment.
- **Opothleyahola and the Creek division** (REPO-IT; not re-read).

### Conflicts

1. **Alabama**: AAC prints "ayes 61; noes 89", which cannot be right; 61-39 is the usual figure (not verified here).
2. **Texas convention vote**: AAC "ayes 106, nays 7" on "the 5th"; TSHA (fetch paraphrase) 166-8 on 1 Feb; the ordinance itself is dated 1 Feb. **Texas referendum**: AAC 34,794-11,285 "in eighty counties"; TSHA 44,317-13,020; the 46,153-14,747 total often cited was not read.
3. **North Carolina**: AAC narrative "On the 21st the ordinance of secession was passed"; the ordinance's own line reads "Done at Raleigh, 20th day of May".
4. **Arkansas admission**: passed 18 May (JCCS; AAC "admitted"), approved 20 May (CSAL).
5. **Kentucky neutrality**: House resolution 16 May (ARCH) against "17th" (AAC); Governor's proclamation 20 May (both); Kentucky special congressional election 20 June (calendar inference; the brief) against AAC's "30th of June".
6. **Missouri**: ordinance vacating offices 30 Jul (ARCH) against 31 Jul (MoSA); Gamble elected 31 Jul (ARCH), "appointed" (MoSA), 1 Aug (other sources), "mid-July" (Hall of Governors); Jackson's flight and Lyon's entry 13 Jun (MoSA) against 15 Jun (ARCH, citing OR I-3 pp.12-14).
7. **Maryland**: July session begins 30 Jul (MSA) against 31 Jul (AAC); vote on secession 13-53 (AAC, undated) against 53-12 (a snippet); Butler "Monday [12th instant]" in OR I-2 p.29 against 13 May by the calendar.
8. **Sixth Massachusetts**: NPS-CDW says it arrived "the next day" after the 18 Apr arrivals, consistent with 19 Apr (the riot day); another summary's "20 April" is not supported.
9. **Washita**: the OR's editorial "[April 16]" is for Emory's departure; REPO-IT's "left about 15-16 April" is consistent. Sturgis on 30 Apr "reported for duty" to Emory at Washita (OR I-1 p.650) while REPO-IT notes it cannot reconcile that with Emory's report of leaving on 15-16 Apr.
10. **Virginia referendum**: the 125,950-20,873 figure is the Governor's proclamation with 34 counties missing (AAC); other tallies in print differ slightly.

### Findings outside scope

- The brief says Kentucky's August elections are "after window"; AAC prints "Confederate Congress, on August 7th" and the legislature's 2 Sep session, which fit.
- Page text of AAC and several NPS pages contains boilerplate advertising and link text; none attempted to direct me.
- The CSAL index lists the Cherokee treaty's page as "334" (an OCR error: Seminole is p.332, Quapaw p.376, Seneca p.374; the Cherokee text begins after p.380).
- The Confederate Congress's acts on the Indian nations: the **Act for the protection of certain Indian tribes** (approved 21 May 1861) is in CSAL and was "passed" by 17-20 May in JCCS; the repository's Indian Territory note does not cite this act as a game event. The act is an obvious scenario hook (the Confederacy's offer of protectorate precedes every treaty).
- `docs/research/2026-11-15-indian-territory-1861-sources.md` section 4 reads "Cobb's troops were gone before 9 May"; that matches OR I-1 p.648 (Emory found them on 9 May, northeast of the post). The note's "Washita left about 15-16 April and occupied from Texas by 16-17 April" is consistent with the OR's bracket.


## Appendix B: Men under arms and planned operations (helper N2)


Quality tags: `return` = a dated official count; `estimate` = a stated estimate; `inferred` = my reading or arithmetic; `snippet-unverified` = seen only in a search summary.

### How the sources were read (read this first)
- **LIV** = Livermore, *Numbers and Losses in the Civil War in America* (1900). I downloaded the archive.org OCR (`numberslosses00liverich`) and read the pages myself. Page numbers are the book's running heads (OCR digits can garble). Livermore cites the OR by SERIAL number: "2 W. R." = serial 2 = OR I-2; "5 W. R." = OR I-5; "127 W. R." = serial 127 = OR IV-1.
- **OR pages "via civilwar.com"** were read through the WebFetch tool at civilwar.com's one-page-per-URL transcription. The tool returns a **paraphrase, not the text**: every number marked "(via civilwar.com)" is the tool's rendering and should be re-checked against the printed page before it carries weight. Page numbers match the printed OR where I could test them (the I-2 page ids are sequential; I-2 p.67 was Morris's Philippi order, p.77 the start of Big Bethel, as expected). Mirror page numbers can drift by one.
- **NPS** = nps.gov Civil War pages. The state tables (`nps.gov/civilwar/westvirginia.htm`, `missouri.htm`, `maryland.htm`, `southcarolina.htm`, `florida.htm`) reproduce **Dyer's *Compendium* (1908)** engagement lists with Dyer's Union losses (header says so). The CWSAC battle-detail pages (`search-battles-detail.htm?battleCode=...`) give estimated strengths and casualties for the "principal battles"; several carry placeholder zeros or obvious errors (noted below).
- I could NOT get the OR I-2, I-3, I-4 or IV-1 raw text (the archive.org identifiers for Series I vols 1-14 are not under the `rootrich` pattern; Dyer's full OCR download truncated). So no figure from Walker's reports, the Confederate Secretary of War, or OR IV-1 was read directly; see Gaps.

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### A3. Bands for about 31 July 1861 (no averages)

**Union (US Army, volunteers, three-months men; not the Navy).**
- **Low:** 152,354 aggregate present and absent, partial returns of 26 Jun - 2 Jul 1861 (OR III-1 p.301; excludes Annapolis and Kentucky, part of Ohio, unattached men, recruits and forming regiments). Same abstract: 108,440 present for duty.
- **Anchor (1 Jul):** 186,751 aggregate (183,588 present) on the PMG/Livermore/Fox series.
- **High:** about 310,000 (OR III-1 p.304) or 307,875 (AAC) "force at command" on 1 Jul, which includes about 50,000-60,000 men in 55 accepted regiments and 25,000 regulars not yet raised.
- **My reading of the "in service on 31 Jul" figure** (inferred, not a source number): the 1 Jul aggregate 186,751 plus some or all of the 55 accepted regiments that AAC says entered service within about 20 days (about 50,000-60,000), less three-months men discharged in the last days of July: roughly **190,000 to 245,000**. Treat as a working bracket only; no dated return for 31 Jul exists in the sources read.
- **Conflicts:** (i) 186,751 (PMG) vs 152,354 (AG abstract) on the same date: explained by the abstract's stated omissions; (ii) 310,000 / 80,000 (OR p.304 as paraphrased) vs 307,875 / 77,875 (AAC); (iii) 129,894 printed vs 147,736 summed (OR p.301); (iv) 20,304 vs 20,334 regulars (OR p.699 as paraphrased, Dec 1861).

**Confederate (Provisional Army plus state forces).**
- **Fighting armies, 21 Jul:** 32,232 effectives in the Army of the Potomac, the Army of the Shenandoah and Holmes's brigade (LIV p.77), 33,752 present for duty in the same two armies (LIV p.42).
- **Whole Confederacy, July 1861:** about 112,040 (LIV p.42), which Livermore says is likely too low; other dated official totals for July were not found (Walker/Cooper/IV-1 not read).
- **Authorised/called:** 70,000 called (Feb-Mar); 100,000 authorised 6 Mar; the 8 May act accepted volunteers for the war (LIV p.30, p.51).
- **Band stated:** low 32,232 (effectives of the two Manassas/Valley armies only; not a national figure), whole-Confederacy about 112,040 (compiled; likely under-counted). No upper bound from a primary source was reached.
- **Union:Confederate ratio on the dates LIV pairs:** Livermore's own table gives 112,040 / 186,751 = 60% for July 1861 (the table prints "60.7" and the two-date average 231,729 / 381,334). Source for the ratio: LIV p.47.

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

## PART B. Planned operations fought, 5 Mar - 31 Jul 1861

"Planned" = a plan by one side's commander with an objective; "side planned" is my reading of the document named (`inferred` where marked). Losses are the side's own report or Dyer's (via NPS) as stated; Livermore gives strengths and losses only for First Bull Run in this window (his battle tables on pp.76-115 begin with Bull Run, then Wilson's Creek on 10 Aug).
