Other events in the OR I-2 pp.1-3 and Dyer lists that I did **not** classify as planned operations: Baltimore riot 19 Apr (a mob attack on passing troops: Dyer, 4 killed, 20 wounded = 24; no commander's plan), Harpers Ferry burned 18 Apr and evacuated 15 Jun-15 Jul (withdrawals), Norfolk Navy Yard 20 Apr, Pensacola/Fort Pickens relief arrival 17 Apr (no fight), the Maryland/Potomac minor skirmishes (Seneca Mills 14 Jun, Conrad's Ferry 17 Jun, Goose Creek/Edwards Ferry 18 Jun: Dyer lists 1 killed, 4 wounded in one), Great Falls 7 Jul (2 killed), numerous Missouri skirmishes (Dyer, Monroe Station 11 Jul 3 killed; Fulton 17 Jul 16; Blue Mills 24 Jul 13).

### B2. Counts (planned operations fought, my reading)

| Definition | Union-planned | Confederate-planned | Disputed or both | Total |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Livermore-scale** (Livermore gives strengths and losses: battles pp.76-115): in the window **First Bull Run only** | 1 (McDowell's offensive; the Confederate counter-plan is the same day) | 0 | 0 | **1** |
| **Battle only, NPS CWSAC "principal battles" dated in the window** (Fort Sumter I; Sewell's Point; Aquia Creek; Big Bethel; Philippi; Hoke's Run; Boonville; Carthage; Rich Mountain; Blackburn's Ford; Manassas I) | 8 or 9 (Sewell's Point, Aquia Creek, Big Bethel, Philippi, Hoke's Run, Boonville, Rich Mountain, Manassas I; plus Blackburn's Ford if a reconnaissance in force counts as a planned operation) | 1 (Fort Sumter) | 1 (Carthage) | **11** |
| **Battle only, excluding the three tiny naval exchanges and the reconnaissance** (Sumter, Big Bethel, Philippi, Hoke's Run, Boonville, Carthage, Rich Mountain, Manassas I: the eight with ground troops and a stated plan) | 6 (Philippi, Big Bethel, Hoke's Run, Boonville, Rich Mountain, Manassas I) | 1 (Sumter) | 1 (Carthage) | **8** |
| **Battle plus planned skirmish/affair** (the 26 rows of B1, excluding #13 for which no plan or fight is read, and counting #11 with #12 and #26 as one) | about 20 | 3 (Sumter, Cole Camp [inferred], Mesilla) | 1 (Carthage) | about **24** |

How the count moves: the Confederate side plans almost nothing in the window beyond the Sumter bombardment (12 Apr) and Baylor's New Mexico stroke (23-27 Jul); every other fight where the planner is identified was brought by the Union side (Lyon in Missouri, McClellan and Morris in western Virginia, Butler at Fort Monroe, Patterson on the Potomac, McDowell at Bull Run). The "all fought engagements" count does not need a Confederate planned operation; where the Confederates planned something it was a counter-plan on the day (Beauregard at Bull Run, Magruder's defence at Bethel, Jackson at Hoke's Run). The numbers above are a count of the record's cases and are not a required event.

### B3. Gaps and conflicts specific to Part B
- Strengths are given only where an OR page or Livermore stated them (Big Bethel, Carthage, Mesilla, Hoke's Run Confederate, Bull Run); Philippi, Rich Mountain, Vienna, Fairfax C.H., Alexandria, Scary Creek and the naval fights have placeholder zeros in CWSAC or no number read.
- **CWSAC/Dyer conflicts on losses:** Hoke's Run (Dyer US 23; CWSAC US 73, CS 25); Boonville (Dyer US 19; CWSAC 31); Rich Mountain (McClellan 11/35 then 13/40; Dyer 12 + 59 = 71; CWSAC US 74); Carrick's Ford (Dyer 13 + 40 duplicates McClellan's Rich Mountain totals); Carthage (Dyer US 76; CWSAC US 44); Manassas: CWSAC prints "CS 3,230" engaged, plainly an error against LIV's 32,232.
- Vienna 17 Jun: not read; the OR I-2 p.2-3 list shows Vienna events on 9 and 17 Jul.
- Plan documents for #4, #6, #8, #13, #15 were not read.

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Gaps (needs-source)
1. **Confederate Secretary of War Walker's reports, Confederate Adjutant General (Dec 1861, 127 W.R. 823, 963), OR IV-1 and OR I-51 returns:** not read directly. Searches did not surface them; civilwar.com's Series IV pages were not located; the OR I-51 pt.2 pages I opened (pp.180, 197; I-52 pt.2 p.117) held no strength figures. Next step: OR IV-1 (serial 127) pp.117-135, 302, 765-825, 963.
2. **Cameron's report of 1 Jul 1861 as the Senate printed it:** Pitt digital library copy (`digital.library.pitt.edu/islandora/object/pitt:31735054851682`) is bot-blocked; the OR III-1 pp.301-305 reprint was read only as paraphrase.
3. **No dated Union or Confederate return for 31 Jul 1861** found. Army of the Potomac / Division of the Potomac returns for early Aug (OR I-5, serial 5) were not reached.
4. **Navy** at 31 Jul: no number; Marine Corps strength not found. Welles's report of 4 Jul 1861 and the Dec 1861 report (Serial Set) needed.
5. **Raw OR text:** archive.org full-text for OR I-2, I-3, I-4 not obtained; Dyer's OCR download truncated. ehistory.osu.edu returned 403 (not routed around); census.gov, HathiTrust not tried.
6. OR p.301 internal sum (129,894 vs 147,736) and OR p.304 (310,000 vs AAC 307,875) need the page image.

### Conflicts (summary)
- 21,883 (LIV) vs 21,833 (OR I-2 p.487, via mirror) vs 21,863 (OR I-2 p.568 aggregate, via mirror) for the Confederate Army of the Potomac, 21 Jul.
- 186,751 (PMG, 1 Jul) vs 152,354 (AG abstract, c. 30 Jun) vs 235,000 "in service" (OR p.304) for the Union on about the same date; their definitions differ (see A3).
- 310,000 / 80,000 (OR III-1 p.304) vs 307,875 / 77,875 (AAC).
- Carthage planner: Sigel (US report) vs Jackson (CWSAC).
- Several loss figures (see B3).

### Findings outside scope
- Cameron to Curtin, 11 Jul 1861 (OR III-1 p.325): three-month regiments expire in July; the department wanted the field force kept up. Evidence for the "expiring terms" mechanism around First Bull Run (Patterson's army especially).
- Jordan's Confederate estimate of the Union at Bull Run, 54,140 (OR I-2 p.569), is about double Livermore's 30,594 present for duty: the Confederate command overestimated the Union army.
- Lincoln's proclamation of 3 May 1861 raised the Army and Navy ceilings 22,714 and 18,000 before Congress acted (Act of 29 Jul 1861, 25,000 regulars).
- Fox's regimental-losses mirror table shows 1 Jan 1861 = 16,367 aggregate, the same as the 16,367 quoted by a search summary of the Proclamation text; useful baseline for the "army of 16,000" trope.
- A fetched page (spirit61 Big Bethel post) contained only blog analysis; no instruction text appeared on any fetched page.


## Appendix C: Pushback cases S01 to S21 (helper N3)


Evidence file for lane 1.10 (research helper N3). Read-only: nothing in the repository was edited. Situations file read: `docs/design/SITUATIONS-1861-62.md` S01 to S21 in full; research notes read: [MF] march-1861-facts (in full), [SW] secession-winter-1861 (sections 2.1 to 2.5, 4.3, 4.4, 4.6), line hits in [UE] and [IF].

### Method and honesty notes

- **Types** used (the game's): CR = compliance with reservations; SW = slow walk (delay); OBJ-O = objection to an order; OBJ-P = objection to a plan; REF = refusal; RELAY = relay objection or relay hold; FILTER = filter hold or trim. Plain compliance is not a pushback case and is not counted.
- **Quality codes**: `verified primary` (VP) = I read a page of the Official Records, ORN, a document transcribed from the original, or a reported-case text that carries the act and date. `verified secondary` (VS) = I read a reputable secondary page (university or museum project, Burlingame via Dickinson House Divided, Epperson chronology, Tulane Sumter site) or only part of the claim is primary. `note-only` (NO) = I could not reach a page; the note's own citation is kept.
- **The fetch tool paraphrases.** Quotations below marked "verbatim" were returned by the tool as verbatim text under 25 words; everything else is paraphrase. OR page numbers come from civilwar.com running heads (can drift one page).
- **Source mirrors used**: civilwar.com per-page OR (page id = serial offset + page: OR I-1 offset 150560; OR I-2 offset 151251; OR I-3 offset 152263; OR III-1 offset 268650); archive.org full text of ORN I-4 (read by character offset); spirit61.info transcriptions of OR telegrams; jfepperson.org and Tulane Sumter site (transcriptions of Lincoln-era documents); en.wikisource.org (Ex parte Merryman); Dickinson House Divided; Mariners' Museum.
- **Failed fetches**: Burlingame vol. 2 ch. 22 (knox.edu PDF) returned "socket hang up" twice, so every claim that rests only on Burlingame stays `note-only`. The archive.org ORN I-4 text I could reach ends near p. 260, so the Gosport pages (ORN I-4 pp. 272-313) were not reachable. A direct curl download of the ORN text produced no file (no workaround attempted).
- **Counting caveat** (also at the end): one person's refusal and the same event's relays are separate rows (e.g. N3-12 Adams and N3-11 the Army staff delay on the same Pickens order). Row N3-03 stands for up to five cabinet members.

| X-06 | S11 | Commodore McCauley | Welles's 10 Apr order to prepare the Merrimack: telegram of 11 Apr that reviving her dismantled engines would take a month | OBJ-O | 11 Apr 1861 | Mariners' Museum, "Gosport in Crisis" (no ORN pages cited): https://www.marinersmuseum.org/2020/04/gosport-in-crisis/ | VS | ORN I-4 p. 272 ff. not reached |
| X-07 | S11 | Commodore McCauley | Welles's order to save the Merrimack: Isherwood had steam up 18 Apr; McCauley, as ranking officer, kept the ship in port (fires banked) | REF | 18 Apr 1861 | same Mariners' Museum page | VS | a search summary says the motive is disputed (channel obstructions vs caution); ORN not reached |

#### Seen and not counted (no order or out of window)

- S02: Lincoln inferred the troops had landed from the Brooklyn's move (a wrong report, not pushback) [MF des. 7].
- S04 / S05: the leaks (Seward, per Burlingame, NO) and Lamon's promise at Charleston (25 Mar) are agents acting beyond a mandate, not a response to an order.
- S06: Welles learned of the Powhatan switch about midnight on 6 Apr (Welles 1871); Foote relayed Seward's telegram and reported (compliance).
- S12: Hicks, Brown and Kane's bridge burning the night of 19 Apr, each blaming another (MSA exhibit case 4: https://msa.maryland.gov/msa/educ/exhibits/hicks/html/case4.html): an act against federal interest, no order.
- S13: Ohio governor and the Massachusetts legislature declining Black volunteers; Cameron's reply to Dodson: no order was given, wording not found [SW 6.4]. Not counted.
- S15: McClellan's 26 May proclamation in his own words: a commander's own text, not an order refused.
- S17: commanders returning fugitives. I read Harney's letter of 14 May (OR I-3 p. 373: fugitives "returned to their owners") but no order existed before Cameron's 30 May letter. Not counted.
- S20: Vallandigham's 10 July speech: opposition, not an order.
- **Out of window (after 31 July) but cited by S16 and S19, excluded from counts**: Magoffin's protest at Camp Dick Robinson, 19 Aug 1861 (note-only); Stringham and Butler seeking credit and Stringham asking to be relieved, 18 Sep 1861; the planning board's advice against taking Hatteras, Aug-Sep 1861.

### Counts by type

Rows are cases, not persons. **Table 1 only** (the catalogue's 34 rows) and **Table 1 plus extras** (41 rows).

| type | Table 1: verified (VP+VS) | Table 1: all | Table 1+extras: verified | Table 1+extras: all |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| plain compliance | not counted | not counted | not counted | not counted |
| CR compliance with reservations | 1 (N3-26) | 2 (+N3-15) | 2 (+X-04) | 3 |
| SW slow walk (delay) | 2 (N3-10, N3-34) | 3 (+N3-28) | 2 | 3 |
| OBJ-O objection to an order | 0 | 0 | 1 (X-06) | 1 |
| OBJ-P objection to a plan | 10 (N3-01, 02, 03, 05, 14, 22, 23, 25, 32, 33) | 13 (+N3-04, 08, 31) | 11 (+X-05) | 14 |
| REF refusal | 10 (N3-12, 13, 16-21, 24, 29) | 11 (+N3-30) | 13 (+X-01, X-02, X-07) | 14 |
| RELAY relay objection or hold | 2 (N3-11, N3-27) | 2 | 2 | 2 |
| FILTER filter hold or trim | 2 (N3-06, N3-07) | 3 (+N3-09, adversary) | 3 (+X-03) | 4 |
| **total** | **27** | **34** | **34** | **41** |

Verified primary only (VP): Table 1 has N3-02, 05, 06, 12, 13, 14, 16, 17, 18, 19, 20, 21, 25, 26, 29, 32, 33, 34 (18 rows: 6 OBJ-P (02, 05, 14, 25, 32, 33), 9 REF (12, 13, 16-21, 29), 1 CR (26), 1 SW (34), 1 FILTER (06)); extras VP: X-01 REF, X-02 REF, X-03 FILTER, X-04 CR.

**Caveats for reading these as lower bounds**
- A row is one act. Persons: N3-03 is five people (Seward, Cameron, Welles, Smith, Bates) with Chase and Blair on the other side; if counted by person the OBJ-P verified count rises by 4 at the group level, but each person's own answer is note-only.
- The Pickens order is one chain with at least four separate rows (N3-10 Scott, N3-11 staff relay, N3-12 Adams, plus N3-13 on the Powhatan edge); the rule that one person's refusal and the same event's relays are counted separately has been applied.
- Governors' refusals (six) are six rows from six seats, each to the same War Department order. They are all REF.
- Only 5 March to 31 July events are counted; the window ends before S16 stage B, S19's board reports and S22 onward.
- Types are my judgement where marked (N3-11 could be SW; N3-26 and N3-28 are nearest-type labels; N3-09 is an adversary's filter).

### Gaps (needs-source)

- Burlingame vol. 2 ch. 22 (https://www.knox.edu/documents/LincolnStudies/BurlingameVol2Chap22.pdf) could not be fetched (socket hang up x2): N3-08, N3-15 and the "Scott ignored the 9 March directive" reading in N3-10 stay note-only. Also ch. 23 appears only through the Dickinson page.
- ORN I-4 pp. 272-313 (Norfolk yard, McCauley, Welles's 10 and 16 Apr orders, Isherwood and Alden): the archive.org text I could read stops near p. 260. Needs-source: https://archive.org/details/officialrecordso0004unse (later pages) or HathiTrust (blocked).
- Welles to McCauley 16 Apr and McCauley's telegrams of 11 and 17-19 Apr: not read in primary.
- Seward's written dissent of 29 Mar and the other members' 15-16 Mar answers (Tulane pages not located; Hesitation/HMar15.html returned 404).
- Scott's 12 Mar letter to Lincoln (its own transcription page not read).
- Harris's telegram of 17 Apr ("not a single man") and Delaware's Governor Burton: no OR page found; Burton's response remains unverified.
- Rector's text in OR I-1 p. 687 vs OR III-1 p. 99 (two versions): only III-1 read.
- Summers and Baldwin: Baldwin's 1866 testimony (Report of the Joint Committee on Reconstruction) not reached.
- Cameron's refusals to Congress: the Globe resolutions and Cameron's replies, with dates (37th Cong. 1st sess.), not reached.
- McDowell's warning: his JCCW testimony (1863) not reached; and OR I-2 pp. 720-ish memoranda of June-July.
- Lyon's own pushback and Harney's relief order's travel: no OR page showing Blair's discretion; Lyon's General Orders No. 5 not read.
- census.gov, HathiTrust, Library of Congress: not used.

### Conflicts

- Adams's refusal: 31 March (situations note S06) vs letters dated 1 April (ORN I-4 pp. 109-110, Welles 1871); Vogdes received Scott's order 31 March.
- Pickens landing night: 12 Apr (Adams, OR I-1 p. 376) vs 11 Apr (Battlefields, per SW).
- Powhatan sailing: 2:45 p.m. (Foote's telegram, ORN I-4 p. 237) vs 2:30 p.m. (Foote's report, p. 238).
- Governors: Magoffin 15 Apr (OR) vs 17 Apr (one source in SW); Harris 17 Apr telegram (blog) vs 20 Apr letter (OR); SW range "15-17 April" for the six; Rector 22 Apr, not 15-17.
- Scott's 28 March memorandum: dated 28 Mar by the page, but its reply to the 15 Mar queries and Hoogenboom's earlier dating leave open that Lincoln knew of its content before 28 Mar.
- Cabinet tally on 15-16 Mar: five-two (Tulane's Chase "yes") vs McPherson's five-against with Blair alone for.
- Blockade positions: note S10 puts Welles for an undeclared blockade and Bates for closing ports; Burlingame via Dickinson has Welles for closing ports (a majority with him) and Bates against a blockade as an act of war (N3-22, 23).
- Harney's relief: S.O. 135 dated 16 May; Harney says received 30 May (31 May letter, blog); one chronology says formally relieved 29 May; Lyon's General Orders No. 5 31 May.
- Gosport: McCauley's own report (decided 19 Apr, began 20 Apr, Paulding's instructions came after) vs the note's "destroyed 20 April against the 16 April order".
- Merryman: the note's "opinion 1 June" vs Wikisource text "in the course of this week" (28 May oral ruling).

### Findings outside scope

- The ORN I-4 p. 112 letters show Porter writing to Foote on 5 Apr: "Meigs thinks Mr. Welles's telegram is bogus" (verbatim per the tool), i.e. a Welles telegram sent to stop Porter was disbelieved: a second refusal path on the same Powhatan edge (not counted: only the one Porter-to-Seward reply is dated and quoted).
- OR III-1 p. 77 shows indorsements "Accept no cavalry" (16 Apr) and p. 76 "Reply: Yes" to an artillery offer: the War Department filtering what states offer, a pattern for a filter-trim band.
- Cameron to Gov. Curtin, 16 Apr (OR III-1 p. 77, paraphrase): the President has reduced Pennsylvania's requisition from 16 regiments to 14, a quota edited in the relay (not pushback by a person).
- The Tulane Sumter site gives a different Porter quotation from ORN p. 239 (Roe relays Porter's "regrets that the dispatch came to his hands so late"); ORN p. 112 carries the verbatim "I received my orders from the President" telegram. Both fit; Roe's note is 5 p.m. on 6 Apr.
- Text on fetched pages was data only; nothing tried to instruct me.


## Appendix D: Union stores (helper N4)


Text base: archive.org item `warofrebellionco0001vari` (OR Series I vol. 1, GPO 1880, OCR full text, read locally as `warofrebellionco0001vari_djvu.txt`). Page numbers here are the printed page numbers read from the OCR running heads; the OCR heading precedes each page's text, and I confirmed p.368 begins "would recommend their being at once abandoned" against civilwar.com id 150928 (so civilwar.com p.N = id 150560+N holds for these pages). No drift found at pp.368-396.

### (a) What OR I-1 pp.368-394 actually hold

They are NOT a statement of provisions at posts and NOT a Commissary General's return. They are the middle of Chapter IV ("Operations in Florida", "Correspondence, etc.-Union"): the letters of the Pickens relief expedition of Col. Harvey Brown and Capt. M. C. Meigs, 3-25 April 1861 (steamers Atlantic and Illinois, the Powhatan, Brooklyn, Sabine, St. Louis, Wyandotte, Crusader), plus Maj. French/Fort Taylor and Arnold/Fort Jefferson orders, Slemmer's report of the bribery attempt, and Meigs's report to Totten (25 Apr, pp.393-397). They contain NO table of stores at 40 blocks. Stores appear only in the five places below; everything else on these pages is guns, horses, troops, coal, and movements.

| Page | Document | What it says about stores | Block |
|---|---|---|---|
| p.380-381 | Brown to Keyes, Fort Pickens, 19 Apr 1861 | describes the post on arrival (17 Apr) as with "the subsistence nearly exhausted" (read in OCR; phrase as printed: "the subsistence nearly exhausted"); no quantity | fb_us_pickens_garrison (state at 17-19 Apr) |
| p.388 | Slemmer to Adams, Fort Pickens, 12 Apr 1861 (enclosed in Slemmer's report of 18 Apr) | asks that the fort be re-enforced; "Provisions should also be landed while there is yet time to do so by the Wyandotte." No quantity | fb_us_pickens_garrison |
| p.386 | Meigs to Brown, Atlantic off Santa Rosa, 20 Apr 1861 | "The soldiers will have six months' supplies as soon as the Illinois is discharged." (read in OCR) | the Brown relief force (no block id in CSV) |
| p.393-394 | Meigs to Totten, Havana, 25 Apr 1861 | the Atlantic carried "supplies for six months for 1,000 men" for the two ships (Atlantic + Illinois), "not less than fifty days' rations of any single article of subsistence" and thirty days' forage for the horses aboard the Atlantic (sailed 7 Apr) | no block id in CSV (the Brown expedition is not a row) |
| p.397 | same | Illinois arrived night of 19-20 Apr with 295 men and "a full cargo of stores" | no block id |

All dates are after 5 March; the checkpoint is 31 July. These bear on a post-window Pickens state (relief force), not on the 4 March starting values.


### Other OR I-1 stores statements found (read in the same OCR text; page = printed page from the nearest running head)

#### Sumter (fb_us_sumter_garrison)
- OR I-1 p.2: Anderson to Thomas, 26 Dec 1860 (received AGO 29 Dec): "about four months' supply of provisions for my command" (read; OCR). Before the window.
- OR I-1 p.141 (Black to Scott, 16 Jan 1861): says Anderson is "supplied with provisions which will last him very well for two months" (an outside estimate by a cabinet member, not a return).
- OR I-1 p.230, Anderson to Thomas, 1 Apr 1861: "could make the provisions last until after the 10th of this month" on short allowance (told to Mr. Fox); "If the governor permits me to send off the laborers we will have rations enough to last us about one week longer." The same letter says the supply, had issues been limited to his command, "would... have lasted for a longer period than that mentioned in my letter of December 26, 1860."
- OR I-1 p.231, Hall (2nd Lt, 1st Artillery, A.A.C.S.) to Anderson, 1 Apr 1861 (inclosure): lists provisions sold to Capt. Foster for the Engineer employes, expressed in rations: pork 5.5 bbl = 1,467 rations; flour 20 bbl = 3,485; hard bread 180 lb = 180; beans 2.5 bu = 1,000; coffee 174 lb = 1,740; sugar 774 lb = 5,160. Days those would have added: pork 16 27/90; flour and hard bread 40 65/90; beans 11 1/9; coffee 19 1/3; sugar 57 1/3. Then: "Or, with what is now on hand, at least thirty-five days of comfortable subsistence for the command, including the laundresses, who were sent away about two months ago." [computed by me: each ration count divided by its day figure gives 90, so the command was reckoned at 90 rations a day.] The 35-day sentence is AMBIGUOUS (total if the engineer issues had not been made, or on hand). It sits against Anderson's same-day "about one week": see Conflicts.
- OR I-1 p.232, Anderson to Thomas, 3 Apr 1861 (No. 92): "Our bread will last four or five days" (read in OCR).
- OR I-1 p.235, Cameron to Anderson, 4 Apr 1861: Fox's information was that Anderson "could hold out till the 15th instant without any great inconvenience"; also hope that he can sustain himself "till the 11th or 12th instant".
- OR I-1 p.243 (Confederate side, hearsay): a telegraph is said to have reported Anderson with "no fuel and but fifteen days' provisions" (one OCR line read, no date read); weak, not used for a number.

#### Key West (fb_us_key_west_garrison): checked, confirmed
- OR I-1 p.343: Brannan (Capt, 1st Artillery, Fort Taylor) to Deas, 15 Jan 1861: "I have four months' provisions and 70,000 gallons water" (read in OCR; header on the page reads "January 15, 1861"). The repository's 120 days is 4 x 30 (a conversion, flag `computed`).

#### Tortugas / Fort Jefferson (fb_us_tortugas_garrison)
- OR I-1 pp.343-348 (Brannan, Arnold, Jan 1861 reports): scanned for provisions, rations, stores, supply, months, days, coal and water: no statement of provisions. NOTHING. pp.371-372 (Brown's 11 Apr orders) and p.376 (Brown, 15 Apr): guns, powder, shells only.

#### Fort Pickens garrison (fb_us_pickens_garrison)
- OR I-1 p.341 (Slemmer to Cooper, Fort Pickens, 5 Feb 1861): "I have provisions for five months for my present command; also a sufficient quantity of water." (read in OCR). Report dated 5 Feb.
- OR I-1 p.357 (Vogdes, c. 7 Feb): "There are plenty of provisions for the present" (no quantity).
- OR I-1 pp.361-362 (Slemmer to Thomas, 18 Mar 1861): contractor refused fresh beef for want of funds; "The United States is indebted to him for three months' supply" (a debt, not a stock); recommends that "subsistence stores be sent immediately" if reinforced.
- OR I-1 p.365 (Slemmer, 30 Mar 1861): "Fresh provisions are now denied us." (no quantity).
- OR I-1 p.366 (Scott to Brown, 1 Apr 1861): other steamers to carry "provisions for the garrison of Fort Pickens for six months" (a plan).
- OR I-1 pp.380-381 (Brown, 19 Apr 1861): on his arrival the post had "the subsistence nearly exhausted".
- OR I-1 p.388 (Slemmer to Adams, 12 Apr 1861): "Provisions should also be landed while there is yet time to do so by the Wyandotte." (no quantity).

#### Vogdes's company A, 1st Artillery (fb_us_pickens_vogdes_co_a): checked, confirmed
- OR I-1 p.351 (Thomas to Dimick, 21 Jan 1861): subsistence "not exceeding four months' supply, as the Brooklyn may be willing to receive". p.353 (order to Vogdes, 21 Jan): "The assistant commissary will furnish this command with three months' provisions, it being all that can be transported on the Brooklyn." The embarkation date 23 Jan is the repository's, not re-checked here.

#### Texas (fb_us_texas_*)
- OR I-1 p.535 (Sibley, Major 3rd Infantry, Fort McIntosh, 11 Mar 1861): "I shall march with about twenty-five days' rations for the command, and all the forage on hand"; a march to Fort Brown. PARTIAL MAP to fb_us_texas_3rd_inf: that block is the regiment's 10 companies embarking at Brazos Santiago; Sibley's Fort McIntosh command is a part.
- OR I-1 pp.568-569 (Reeve, 8th Infantry, May 1861): on leaving Fort Bliss "sufficient transportation could be procured to carry subsistence for only forty days"; on 5 May "I had but five days' rations". S92 source correction: these are six Eighth Infantry companies from Forts Bliss, Quitman and Davis (OR I-1 pp.567-568). The nine companies in the February return are intended to embark at Indianola (pp.523-524), not nine companies already there; the earlier claim that Reeve commanded a different body is withdrawn. His later partial-command rations cannot quantify stores for the whole starting bundle.
- OR I-1 p.598 (Scott to Waite, 19 Mar 1861, received 1 Apr): retain "all the subsistence that can be spared from the steamers". No quantity.

#### New Mexico (fb_us_new_mexico_troops)
- OR I-1 p.599 (Loring, Santa Fe, 25 Mar 1861): Texas secession "involved an additional heavy loss to this department of subsistence stores, of transportation, and of funds." No quantity or days. NOTHING numeric.

#### St Louis arsenal troops, Fort Monroe garrison
- OR I-1 Missouri chapter (to end of the volume): every line matching provisions, rations or subsistence stores scanned; nothing for the arsenal garrison. Fort Monroe pp.351-353: only Dimick's order for Vogdes's company. NOTHING for either.


### ORN I-4 (Navy, Gulf and Atlantic coast, January to May 1861): ship stores statements read

Text base: archive.org item `officialrecordso0004unse` (ORN Series I vol. 4, GPO 1896; OCR). Page = printed number from the nearest running head (these can drift by a page). Quotation marks only where I read the words in the OCR.

- ORN I-4 p.85, Adams (Sabine, senior officer off Pensacola) to Toucey, 19 Feb 1861: "The Brooklyn has coal for eight days. She has fifty days' provisions." (read); Sabine "has water for thirty-five days"; he encloses "a list of the number of days' provisions of each kind" on board Sabine and St. Louis. The two lists are NOT printed in the volume (not in the OCR text): needs-source. Same page: "No provisions could be had at Vera Cruz when I sailed. Bread for twenty-five days and some other articles were supplied us by the Cumberland." (Macedonian's visit to Vera Cruz).
- ORN I-4 p.96, Paymaster Looker (Brooklyn) to Adams, 16 Mar 1861: "three weeks' (twenty-one days) provisions on hand for the crew, officers, and troops on board, with the exception of the articles of flour, butter, sugar, tea, and beans"; "Eighty-three rations stopped in a few days." (read). Matches the repository.
- ORN I-4 p.97, Adams to Welles, 18 Mar 1861: stock "getting very low"; "100 rations stopped on board this ship [Sabine], 83 on board the Brooklyn, and 53 on board the St. Louis"; tea, sugar, coffee, soap, tobacco "entirely out"; the soldiers sent in the Brooklyn "have been living on ship's rations, their own supplies having been landed ... at Fort Pickens"; the storeship Supply, three weeks on passage, would arrive about 1 April, "by which time ... we shall be almost out of provisions"; "about 1,000 people here in danger of starvation". (read, partly paraphrased here).
- ORN I-4 p.100, Walker (Brooklyn) to Adams, 21 Mar 1861: "If we retain four or five days' provisions it will be all that is necessary to take us to Key West. We have five days' coal." And Adams, 22 Mar: "The St. Louis is entirely out of bread and other articles, and by the 1st or 2d of April we shall have eaten up everything on board this ship [Sabine]." (read). Sabine 22 Mar to 1/2 Apr = 10 to 11 days: `computed`.
- ORN I-4 p.105: Adams, 8 Apr: Supply arrived the day before, "twenty-two days from New York"; St. Louis (Poor, 4 Apr) wanted water "before any more provisions are received". No quantities.
- ORN I-4 pp.125-126 (Adams private letter after 12 Apr): at one time "we were so nearly out of provisions that I was obliged to send the Brooklyn after some" (she went to Key West, returning 31 March). No quantity.
- ORN I-4 pp.228-229, Welles private orders, 30 Mar 1861: Pawnee (at Washington yard) and Pocahontas (Norfolk) each to be "in readiness for sea service, on or before the 6th of April" "with one month's stores" (read). Also an order for the Harriet Lane (dated 5 March in the OCR, which prints "March 5b") "with one month's stores"; the Harriet Lane is not a row in the repository's CSV.
- ORN I-4 p.234, Welles to Rowan, 4 Apr 1861: take the Pawnee to Norfolk "for the purpose of receiving a month's supply of provisions" (read). So Pawnee: 30 days, ORDERED, about 5-7 April.
- ORN I-4 p.264, Murray (Pocahontas) to Welles, Hampton Roads, 12 Mar 1861: "We are short of provisions and our upper works leaking" (read; Pocahontas arrived with little coal at Havana on 2 Mar). No quantity. Then the 30 March order above.
- ORN I-4 p.265, Mercer (Powhatan), 13 Mar 1861 (OCR reads "March 13"): arrived New York; "replenished coal" at Havana. No provisions statement. Powhatan was stripped of crew and stores on 1 April (OR I-1 p.394, Meigs; ORN I-4 p.229, "after landing her stores, went out of commission"), recommissioned 5 April, sailed 6 April. No days.
- ORN I-4 p.266, Pendergrast (Cumberland), 25 Mar 1861: she "requires water, stores, and provisions" after 27 days from Vera Cruz (arrived 23 Mar). No quantity.
- ORN I-4 pp.139-140 (Mohawk, Havana, mid-April): arrived Havana "having only two days' water on board"; bought provisions from the Army Subsistence Department at Havana (Capt. A. R. Eddy), no quantity. p.106 (Mohawk, 12 Apr): "seven days' supply" is WATER, not provisions.
- ORN I-4 p.284, Du Pont (19 Apr 1861): the Keystone State carrying marines and seamen to Norfolk was given "two weeks' provisions" and "seven days' coal". A chartered steamer, not a Union block. For fb_us_marines_philadelphia_yard the marine guard "over fifty" went with it: the 14 days belongs to that detachment's voyage, a movement ration, not the yard guard's stock. Record only; do not use as `stores_days`.

ORN I-1 (the 4 March 1861 list and the early-1861 cruiser reports, pp.4-30 area): I searched the whole OCR text for provisions, rations, stores, supplies and coal. For the 1861 foreign and home cruisers (Richmond, Susquehanna, Iroquois, Saratoga, Saranac, Constellation, Seminole, Pulaski, Congress, Mohican, Hartford and others) there is no statement of provisions or stores on hand; the only "provisions" figures in the statistical tables concern later ships (e.g. USS Bainbridge, "Draft: with 4 months' provisions", a loading condition, not a stock).

SecNav Dec 1861 and 4 July 1861 reports: not read for this task in this run (no ship-level provisions expected there; unchecked, so `needs-source`, not `none`).

## DELIVERABLE (b): table (one row per Union block id with something found)

Columns: `id|stores_days|stores_date|basis|sources|quality|notes`. `stated days` = a source states days, months or a ration period. `computed` = my arithmetic. Months converted at 30 days exactly as the repository does for Brannan and Vogdes.

| id | stores_days | stores_date | basis | sources | quality | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| fb_us_sumter_garrison | 7 | 1861-04-01 | stated ("about one week longer" if the laborers are sent away) | OR I-1 p.230 (Anderson to Thomas, 1 Apr 1861) | return (the commander's own written statement) | Repository's 7 is confirmed. Band from the same pages: "4 to 5 days" of bread (p.232, 3 Apr); "after the 10th" on short allowance (p.230); about 35 days "comfortable subsistence" in Hall's A.A.C.S. note (p.231) which is ambiguous; Fox's information, through the 15th (p.235). Hall's ration counts (1,467 pork, 3,485 flour, etc.) give a command of 90 persons/day (`computed`). |
| fb_us_key_west_garrison | 120 | 1861-01-15 | stated "four months' provisions" x 30 days (`computed` conversion) | OR I-1 p.343 (Brannan to Deas, 15 Jan 1861) | return | Repository value and date confirmed; the OCR header on the page reads "January 15, 1861". Nothing later (March to April) was found. |
| fb_us_pickens_garrison | 150 | 1861-02-05 | stated "provisions for five months for my present command" x 30 (`computed` conversion) | OR I-1 p.341 (Slemmer to Cooper, 5 Feb 1861) | return | Repository value confirmed and the report is dated 5 Feb. Later qualitative evidence says the 150 does not carry to 5 March: 18 Mar "recommend that subsistence stores be sent immediately" and the contractor stopped beef (pp.361-362); 30 Mar "Fresh provisions are now denied us" (p.365); 12 Apr Slemmer asks that provisions be landed (p.388); 17-19 Apr Brown found "the subsistence nearly exhausted" (pp.380-381). Vogdes's own supplies were landed here (see next row). |
| fb_us_pickens_vogdes_co_a | 90 | 1861-01-21 (order; embarked 23 Jan per repository) | stated "three months' provisions" x 30 (`computed` conversion) | OR I-1 p.353 (order to Vogdes) and p.351 (Thomas to Dimick: "not exceeding four months' supply") | return (an order stating the issue) | Confirmed. NEW: ORN I-4 p.97 (Adams, 18 Mar 1861): the Brooklyn's soldiers' "own supplies having been landed ... at Fort Pickens" (the greater part), so by mid-March they were living on ship's rations and their 90 days is mostly ashore in the fort, not with the company afloat. The lead should not count it twice with Pickens. |
| fb_us_ship_brooklyn | 21 | 1861-03-16 | stated ("three weeks' (twenty-one days) provisions" for crew, officers and troops, minus flour, butter, sugar, tea, beans) | ORN I-4 p.96 (Looker); also p.85 (Adams, 19 Feb: "fifty days' provisions"); p.100 (Walker, 21 Mar: keep "four or five days'" for Key West) | return | Repository's 21 confirmed. Earlier value 50 on 19 Feb (ship's own, before Adams ordered the excess moved to the Sabine and St. Louis). |

## DELIVERABLE (c): Union block ids for which I found NOTHING usable (stay unknown)

Searched: OR I-1 (every line with provisions, rations, subsistence, commissary, stores, months, days, coal from p.1 to p.658 and the Missouri/Arkansas chapters), ORN I-4 (full OCR text), ORN I-1 (full OCR text). Not read: SecNav reports of Jul/Dec 1861; OR I-50 pt 1 (Pacific; only a web-search listing seen, no page read); OR I-4; ORN II-1.

Army and Marine blocks: fb_us_dc_militia; fb_us_fort_monroe_garrison (only the order to ship Vogdes's stock); fb_us_marines_philadelphia_yard; fb_us_marines_washington_barracks; fb_us_new_mexico_troops (only a qualitative "heavy loss of subsistence stores", OR I-1 p.599); fb_us_pacific_troops (needs OR I-50 pt1 page images or a clean copy); fb_us_st_louis_arsenal_troops; fb_us_texas_troops; fb_us_texas_1st_inf; fb_us_texas_2nd_cav; fb_us_texas_2nd_cav_eg; fb_us_texas_8th_inf (the Reeve battalion figures concern a later partial command); fb_us_texas_3rd_inf (only the partial 25 days above); fb_us_tortugas_garrison; fb_us_washington_regulars; fb_us_pickens_squadron_marines.

Ships with nothing: fb_us_ship_anacostia, congress, constellation, cyane, dacotah, hartford, iroquois, lancaster, michigan, mohican, mystic, narragansett, niagara, portsmouth, pulaski, relief, richmond, saginaw, san_jacinto, saranac, saratoga, seminole, st_marys, sumter, susquehanna, vandalia, water_witch, wyoming. Also (qualitative only, no number): st_louis, cumberland, powhatan, macedonian, crusader, mohawk, supply, wyandotte (see the qualitative table).

## DELIVERABLE (d): conflicts

1. Sumter, 1 Apr 1861: Anderson says "about one week" (OR I-1 p.230) while Hall, his own commissary, closes the list with "at least thirty-five days of comfortable subsistence for the command" (p.231, ambiguous: probably total of what the engineer employes consumed plus what remains, not what remains). Cameron/Fox thought he could last to the 15th (p.235). Anderson, 3 Apr: "Our bread will last four or five days" (p.232). Keep Anderson's 7 (the commander's own, on the day), with the band 4-10 days.
2. Vogdes's three months (OR I-1 p.353) against ORN I-4 p.97 (supplies landed at Pickens): the stock is in the fort by mid-March, so Vogdes's 90 and Slemmer's 150 cannot both be treated as separate stocks for the same men.
3. Slemmer's five months (5 Feb, p.341) against Brown's "subsistence nearly exhausted" (17-19 Apr, pp.380-381). Not strictly a contradiction (the garrison rose from 82 to about 200 with Vogdes and 110 marines), but the dated stock cannot be carried forward to 5 March or later.
4. Powhatan arrival at New York: Foote's report 13 March (ORN I-4 p.226, per the repository) against Mercer's own report: the repository says 18 March, but in my archive.org OCR Mercer's report is dated "March 13, 1861" (ORN I-4 p.265). The OCR may misread an 8 as a 3 (or the repository misread): recheck against a second scan.
5. Sabine's days: the repository's 10 is a computation from "eaten up everything" by 1-2 April; the 19 Feb list of days' provisions is not printed anywhere I could read. No conflict, only an unfilled enclosure.

## Gaps

- ORN I-4 p.85: Adams's two enclosed lists of "days' provisions of each kind" for the Sabine and St. Louis (19 Feb 1861) are not printed in the volume text I read: needs-source (they might sit in the Navy Department records, not published).
- Hall's A.A.C.S. report of 27 January 1861 (the "detailed statement" Anderson mentions on p.230) is not printed in OR I-1 as far as I read: needs-source for Sumter's stocks on 5 March. The 5 March figure itself is not in the Records; the nearest are 26 Dec 1860 (four months) and 1 April (7 days).
- SecNav reports (July and December 1861): not read.
- OR I-50 pt 1 (Pacific) and OR I-4: not read. civilwar.com page ids for OR I-50 pt 1 follow the pattern `civilwar.com/official-record/950-pacific-part-i/<id>-<page>-series-i-volume-l-i-serial-105-pacific-part-i.html` (e.g. p.1 = id 250875, p.461 = 251335); I did not fetch any.
- Blocked or unavailable: none hit; the civilwar.com page fetch tool paraphrased text so I used the archive.org full text for everything quoted.

## Findings outside scope

- The brief's expectation that pp.368-394 give stores for about 40 Union blocks does not hold: they are the Brown-Meigs Pickens expedition correspondence of April 1861 (see (a)). The nearest thing to a stores statement for many blocks at once is the Adams squadron correspondence in ORN I-4 pp.85, 96-100 (Sabine, Brooklyn, St. Louis) and Welles's orders of 30 Mar-4 Apr (Pawnee, Pocahontas, Harriet Lane).
- The repository CSV has no row for the Brown relief expedition (Atlantic, Illinois, Baltic) or for the Harriet Lane, although README says Harriet Lane is one of 44 ships; CSV count in this worktree is 60 Union blocks, 40 ships.
- The OCR reads Meigs's report "Havana, April 25, 1801" and Mohawk's Havana date as "April ID": OCR noise, not a source conflict.
- Page-numbering: printed page = the running head that precedes the text in the OCR; at OR I-1 p.343 the Key West letter sits on p.343 (running head "REPORTS. 343"), not p.342.



## Appendix E: Unknown strengths (helper N5)


Method and caveats (read first)
- Almost all OR pages were read through the civilwar.com per-page transcriptions (page id = 150560 + page for OR I-1; OR III-1 p.N = id 268650+N; OR I-50 pt 1 and I-52 pt 2 have their own ids). The fetch tool paraphrases, so no quotation below is claimed verbatim; short phrases in quote marks were reported by the tool as printed text and are marked "as printed". Page numbers follow OCR running heads and may drift by one.
- Blocked/failed in this run: usmcu.edu (403), apps.dtic.mil (403), civilwartalk.com (403), history.navy.mil DANFS (404/connection refused), navsource.org (DNS error), the civilwarnavy.com PDF "Charleston Naval Yard" (image/binary only), adhc.lib.ua.edu (certificate rejected). HathiTrust/archive.org full texts were not tried (blocked or unavailable per brief).
- Quality key: return / estimate / inferred / snippet-unverified. "Inferred" means my arithmetic on sourced figures, shown in the note.

### Table (one row per block id)

A search summary (civilwarnavy.com PDF, unreadable here) says only 30 enlisted men had been recruited for the S.C. naval force by 6 Apr and that the General Clinch had a detail of 20 militia as marines in Jan 1861: snippet, unverified. No crew complement for Gordon, Lady Davis, Clinch, Charleston, Seabrook, Catawba found.

Not read: OR IV-1 p.178 (Galt) and p.135 (requisition 700), already in repo|snippet-unverified (secondary; Bergeron not read)|The 744 is the whole regiment (all ten companies; the three at Fort Pike and Fort Macomb are not at the forts). 744 x 7/10 = 521 is my arithmetic. N. Luckett, Texas QM and Commissary General, to the C.S. authorities, with Van Dorn orders of 3 May: Ford's regiment, three companies on the Rio Grande (Davidson, Nolan, Littleton), two present (Walker, Pyron), five more to rendezvous on the 10th = ten); OR I-1 p.610 (Walker: C.S. mounted riflemen regiment, ten companies of 60 to 80 men each); OR I-1 p.619 (Texas legislature provided a regiment of mounted riflemen of one thousand men, as printed, writer/date not shown)|inferred|Strength of the State regiment is nowhere stated. The company rule 60-80 is the C.S. regiment's (p.610), not the State regiment's: do not treat it as sourced for Ford. The "one thousand men" regiment (p.619) is not tied to Ford on its page. fb_us_fort_monroe_garrison|about 410 (7 companies x about 59); band 350-450|1861-01-01 (companies); 1860-12-31 (artillery average)|unknown|unknown||OR III-1 p.23 (distribution, 1 Jan 1861: A, C 1st Art; B, L 2nd; F, K 3rd; D, L 4th at Fort Monroe, A left 24 Jan); OR I-2 p.641 (Scott, mid-May: "seven companies of regular artillery" as printed); OR III-1 p.22 (Army abstract 31 Dec 1860: Artillery 126 officers, 2,855 men present for duty, 2,981 aggregate; absent 188)|inferred|No post return found. Seven companies (eight less A, 1st Artillery, gone 24 Jan) is sourced twice (Jan and May). 2,855 men / 48 artillery companies (4 regiments x 12, my assumption) = 59.5 a company; 7 x 59.5 = about 416. Of those, 40 men (5, 6, 6, 6, 5, 6, 6 from C/1st, B/2nd, L/2nd, F/3rd, K/3rd, D/4th, L/4th) were detached to Vogdes's company on 22 Jan (OR I-1 p.352): they left with it, so the band is lowered a little. Note OR III-1 p.23 lists F and K 3rd, where the repo notes say I/3rd: OCR/ordering difference, to check. Dimick's 9/10 May report carries no figure.

Companies: 39 on 1 Jan, less three of the 8th Infantry (E, B, K) that left for Texas by about 21 Feb = 36 on 5 Mar (my count of the printed list; OCR dates uncertain). Regiments: Mounted Rifles (A-I-C-D-E-F-G-H-K, 9 companies in the list), 7th Infantry, 5th Infantry, 1st and 2nd Dragoons, 10th Infantry. The Pacific lost no units between 1 Jan and 30 Jun except moves: Sumner withdrew Oregon/Washington garrisons only from late April, so the 30 Jun return is a fair proxy for the start.

No ORN or Navy Register complement found. Needs: DANFS Anacostia I, ORN I-4 Potomac Flotilla muster, or the Navy Department's 1861 report tables.

fb_us_ship_michigan|88 (listed complement), unverified|undated (design: 1844)|unknown|unknown||Wikipedia infobox USS Michigan (1843): "88 officers and men" no footnote; a search summary of the DANFS entry also gives 88 and one 18-pdr (not read directly: DANFS 404)|snippet-unverified|88 looks like the original 1844 complement, not an 1861 muster. 1861 actual crew not found. Two histories read (Hagen History Center blog; Erie Maritime Museum page) give no crew number.

fb_us_st_louis_arsenal_troops|488-493 (9 officers and 484 men per Harney 19 Feb; aggregate 488 per Hagner 21 Feb)|1861-02-19 and 1861-02-21 (about two weeks before the start)|unknown (ordnance employees are outside the troop command: Harney letter of 13 Mar)|unknown||OR I-1 p.654 (Brig Gen Harney to Col Thomas, 19 Feb: Hagner "re-enforced by some three hundred recruits from Jefferson Barracks", command "now numbers nine officers and four hundred and eighty-four men" as printed; Lyon's company of the 2nd Infantry arrived earlier, not included separately); OR I-1 p.655 (Bvt Maj Hagner to Col Craig, 21 Feb: "Our aggregate this morning is four hundred and eighty eight" as printed; reinforcements 16 Feb 3 officers + 200 men, 19 Feb 1 officer + 101 men; smallpox and measles reported); OR I-1 pp.657-661 (Lyon assumed command of the troops 13 Mar under SO 74, formal transfer from Hagner ordered 6 Apr: no numbers)|return (Hagner: own aggregate) and estimate (Harney)|Two weeks before the start, not on it; no 4-5 March return found. Composition is mostly recruits (about 300) plus Lyon's company plus Hagner's ordnance detachment. Whether the recruits stayed through March is not stated on any page read. Harney's 493 (9+484) and Hagner's 488 differ by five and are two days apart.

### Cross-checks and yardsticks used above
- S.C. volunteer division, 6 Mar: 104 companies, 8,835 rank and file = 85 a company (OR I-1 p.265, Gist). Pickens, 9 Apr: ten companies = 800 men = 80 a company (p.293). Ripley, 5 Mar: Co D artillery battalion present 74 (p.262). These support 74-85 a company for S.C. units and are the only basis for the Charleston inferred bands.
- Charleston totals found (not requested but useful): Pickens 9 Apr, 3,700 at posts and batteries, 3,000 more due next day (p.293); Beauregard 8 Apr limited to 5,000 men by instructions of 1 March (p.290); Pickens (undated, p.293): 1,950 on Morris Island.
- Army-wide 31 Dec 1860 present for duty (OR III-1 p.22 as read by the tool; the tool flagged garbled OCR): Cavalry 82 officers 3,123 men; Artillery 126/2,855; Infantry 164/7,140 (the 164 uncertain); Engineer 48/63; Ordnance 59/411; total present 745/13,918, aggregate 14,663; absent 371/1,341; present and absent aggregate printed 16,367 but components give 16,375 (printing or OCR discrepancy).

### Ids for which I found NOTHING (searched where)
- fb_st_charleston_2nd_artillery_battalion: OR I-1 pp.33-45, 262-317 all read; no figure.
- fb_st_tx_galveston_batteries: OR I-1 pp.610-611 only guns; no other Texas source found.
- fb_us_marines_philadelphia_yard: no marine station return reachable (usmcu.edu 403); NavyReg1861/ORN not re-read.
- fb_st_charleston_hagood_regiment and fb_st_charleston_rion_battalion: no figure of any date; only inferred bands, which the lead may prefer to leave unsized.
- fb_st_charleston_1st_infantry_regulars and fb_st_charleston_regiment_of_rifles: no regimental figure (only the 318 recruits that may be the same men, and the 47/24-man detachments).
- fb_us_ship_anacostia and fb_us_ship_michigan: only unsourced listed complements (67 and 88); no 1861 muster.
- fb_cs_charleston_guard_steamers: no crew figures (only 75 army men embarked).
- fb_st_tx_ford_regiment: no men-count (ten companies only).

### Conflicts
- St Louis arsenal: Harney 9 + 484 = 493 (19 Feb) vs Hagner 488 aggregate (21 Feb); neither is a 5 March count and the figures include about 300 recruits whose stay is not documented.
- Fort Morgan: Reese (Jan) 400-500 men vs Clemens (4 Apr) nine full companies + 46 men; the same period saw six South Alabama companies leave (J. T. Morgan, 4 Apr). "about 88 men" in Clemens is as printed, probably an OCR/truncation; do not use.
- Pacific: 3,382 (15 Jan, search summary, unverified) vs 3,361 (30 Jun, present and absent, OR III-1 p.301): near-identical figures at dates five and a half months apart suggest a measurement or sourcing mix-up in the secondary summary.
- Marine Corps totals in secondary pieces: 63 officers and 1,712 (Warfare History Network), 63 and 1,892 (a Navy-linked source in search results), 48 and 2,338 (Emerging Civil War, Kolakowski), 63 officers and 1,775 men per the Adjutant and Inspector report of 2 Nov 1860 (search summary of a Marine Corps History article). Not station figures; for the lead's orientation only.
- Fort Monroe companies: OR III-1 p.23 reads F and K 3rd Artillery; the repo note (from OR I-1 pp.351-353) reads I/3rd and K/3rd. Check the page image.
- Charleston: Beauregard's "five thousand men" limit (8 Apr) vs the repo's 3,000 estimate (Walker, 4 Mar): different dates and measures, no direct clash.

### Gaps (needs-source) and blocked URLs
- Marine station returns: Nov 1860 Adjutant and Inspector report; SecNav reports Dec 1860 and 1861; https://www.usmcu.edu/Portals/218/United%20States%20Marines%20at%20Harper_s%20Ferry%20and%20in%20the%20Civil%20War%20PCN%2019000410300.pdf (403); https://www.usmcu.edu/Outreach/Marine-Corps-University-Press/MCH/Marine-Corps-History-Winter-2021/Understanding-Battlefield-Performance-of-US-Marines-Ashore-during-the-Civil-War/ (403); https://apps.dtic.mil/sti/trecms/pdf/AD1177332.pdf (403).
- DANFS Michigan I and Anacostia I (history.navy.mil, 404/connection refused); ORN I-4 pp.260-262 (Charleston guard steamers) not on the mirror used.
- Army Official Register 1861 and Heitman for regimental stations were not consulted (company lists come from OR III-1 pp.22-26 instead).
- Texas Convention ordinance text creating the State regiment (March 1861): not found; State archive (tsl.texas.gov) not searched in depth.
- OR IV-1 pp.135, 178, 192-193, 209 (Alabama and Louisiana requisitions) were not re-read; civilwar.com index for Series IV not located.
- Hagood, Memoirs of the War of Secession (1910): [MEMOIR], not read.
- Lyon's own St Louis count of 11 May is after the start and was not pursued; no 4-5 March arsenal return found.

### Findings outside scope
- OR III-1 pp.22-26 (civilwar.com ids 268672-268676) carry a "Statement showing the distribution of the U.S. Army on 1 Jan 1861, with the changes to 15 Apr 1861", by company and post, with movement dates. It covers every department (Texas, Pacific, New Mexico, East, West). It would let the lead build company counts for every US regular block, and p.22 has the 31 Dec 1860 abstract by arm. Recommend a separate pass to turn it into a block-by-block company table.
- OR III-1 p.301 has the 30 Jun 1861 abstract of returns by department (Pacific, New Mexico, Utah, West, Florida, Washington, etc.) with officers, men, aggregate: useful for the 31 July checkpoint.
- The Gist return of 6 Mar (OR I-1 p.265) gives total S.C. volunteer organisation (8,835), which can bound the Charleston mobilisation.
- Repo repeat: fb_st_charleston_moultrie_infantry_recruits (318) may duplicate the regulars infantry block (see above).
- No page text tried to give instructions to me (only site navigation items such as "Fort Gordon Army Base" sidebar headlines, ignored).


## Appendix F: The Union calls and the state regiments (helper N6)


Method note: all OR III-1 pages read via civilwar.com page mirror (`https://civilwar.com/official-record/990-union-letters-orders-reports/<id>-<page>-series-iii-volume-i-serial-122-union-letters-orders-reports.html`, id = 268650 + page for pp. 67-146; 269250 = p.600). The fetch tool summarises/paraphrases, so figures below are as extracted; text-level checks are noted. Page numbers are the running heads of the mirror (they can drift by a page against the printed volume).

### (a) Table of the calls

| Call | Date | Terms and numbers | Source (read) | Quality |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Proclamation calling militia | 15 Apr 1861 (Washington; Lincoln, countersigned Seward) | Militia of the several States to aggregate 75,000; Act of 28 Feb 1795; Congress summoned for 4 July ("the fourth day of July next"); insurgents to disperse within twenty days | OR III-1 p.67 (proclamation text begins; numbers 75,000) and p.68 (dates, 1795 Act, 4 July, 20 days) | return (official text) |
| War Dept letter/requisition to governors | 15 Apr 1861 (Cameron; signer's name not shown on p.68, closing signed Simon Cameron on p.69) | Infantry or riflemen for three months "unless sooner discharged"; no man under commissioned-officer rank apparently over 45 or under 18; quota table by state (below); governors notified by telegram same day to 24 states | OR III-1 pp.68-69 | return |
| AGO organisation order | 16 Apr 1861 (L. Thomas) | Militia company: 1 captain, 1 first lt, 1 ensign, 4 sgt, 4 cpl, 1 drummer, 1 fifer, 64 privates (regiment = 10 companies; fixed at 780-man regiment per table) | OR III-1 p.76 | return |
| Pennsylvania quota reduced | 16 Apr 1861 (Cameron to Curtin) | President reduced requisition from 16 to 14 regiments; 14 colonels ... 8,960 privates (as printed; 14 x 640) | OR III-1 p.77 and footnote p.69 | return |
| Proclamation (3 May) | 3 May 1861 (Lincoln; "By the President", Seward) | 42,034 volunteers for three years unless sooner discharged (infantry and cavalry); regular army +8 infantry, 1 cavalry, 1 artillery regiments, max aggregate +22,714; 18,000 seamen for 1 to 3 years; to be submitted to Congress when assembled | OR III-1 p.146 (mirror); cross-check American Presidency Project "Proclamation 83" https://www.presidency.ucsb.edu/documents/proclamation-83-increasing-the-size-the-army-and-navy (read; says same numbers) | return (two reads agree) |
| Act of 22 July 1861 (Statutes at Large vol. 12, p.268, ch. 9) | Approved 22 July 1861 | President may accept up to 500,000 volunteers (cavalry, infantry, artillery) for not less than 6 months nor more than 3 years (as read in OR); no new proclamation needed after those already called ("without previous proclamation" per 31 July act sec. 2); volunteers disbanded at end of war; two-year/war service gets $100 bounty; company officers elected | OR III-1 pp.380-383 (printed as G.O. 49, 3 Aug 1861); Statutes citation from govinfo search result: https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/STATUTE-12/pdf/STATUTE-12-Pg268.pdf (scan only; could not OCR in this run) | return for OR text; Statutes page not read (scan) |
| Act of 25 July 1861 | Approved 25 July 1861 | Supplementary: President may accept volunteers up to 500,000 (additional 500,000 in the OR text on p.383), "during the war"; may appoint major and brigadier generals as needed | OR III-1 p.383 | return, but number/terms need the Statutes check (see Conflicts) |
| Act of 31 July 1861 | 31 July 1861 | Sec. 1 reimburse bands of 15 Apr volunteers; Sec. 2 President may accept volunteers under the 22 July act "without previous proclamation"; adds 9 infantry, 1 cavalry, 1 artillery regiment to the regular army (G.O. 48) | OR III-1 p.372 | return |
| Ratification act | 6 Aug 1861 | Congress ratified the acts of the President since 4 March including the 3 May call (per OR p.383 footnote) | OR III-1 p.383 footnote (the table's introduction) | return (as reported by OR editor; act text not read) |

Not yet read: Lincoln's 4 July 1861 message (avalon.law.yale.edu URL tried returned 404); Richardson. See Gaps.

### (b) State-by-state table

Reading key. "3 mo (OR p.699)" = Cameron's annual-report table of 1 Dec 1861, "Three-months'" column, OR III-1 p.699 (quality: return; this is the final count of three-month men by state; the column's printed total is 77,872 but the 19 rows I read sum to 77,875, which is also the figure in the text on p.700; "77,875 immediately obtained"). "fn p.69" = the footnote to the quota table, men furnished "under this call" as finally adjusted by the War Department (total 91,816). "Oct" = governors' replies of 26-28 Oct 1861 to Cameron (OR III-1 pp.598-604). "21 Jun" = replies to Cameron's 21 June 1861 telegram asking each governor how many regiments were organised and mustered "for the war" (OR III-1 pp.284-291). Governor names are given only where a page I read names them. Dates of 3-month units' muster-in are not on these pages (see (c)).

State-by-state "raised for three years by 31 July": no OR III-1 page I read gives a state-by-state table at 31 July. The nearest dated primary returns are the 21-22 June 1861 replies above (pp.285-291), the Secretary of War's report of 1 July 1861 (OR III-1 pp.301-305: 153 volunteer regiments in service and 55 more, "mostly ready", accepted for the war = 208 regiments accepted under the 3 May call; 310,000 at the government's command, of which 80,000 three-month men to be deducted, leaving 230,000 for service), the 26-28 Oct returns, and the 1 Dec table. Treat any state's 31 July regiment count as `needs-source` (Dyer's regimental dates by state would settle it; see Gaps).

#### Cumulative statement attached to the July acts (OR III-1 p.384, footnote to p.383; date of the final figures not on the page: this is a later adjustment, not a 31 July figure)
Quota first number, men furnished second (all calls under the 3 May proclamation and the acts of 22 and 25 July 1861): ME 17,560/18,104; NH 9,234/8,338; VT 8,950/9,508; MA 34,868/32,177; RI 4,955/6,286; CT 13,057/10,865; NY 109,056/120,231; NJ 19,152/11,523; PA 82,825/85,160; DE 3,145/1,826; MD 15,578/9,355; W.Va. 8,497/12,757; DC 1,627/1,795; OH 67,365/84,116; IN 38,832/61,341; IL 47,785/81,952; MI 21,357/23,546; WI 21,753/25,499; MN 4,899/6,937; IA 19,316/21,987; MO 31,544/25,238; KY 27,237/35,095; KS 3,235/6,953; Nebraska "no quota"/91. Term notes under it: most three years; 30,950 NY two-year; one-year 863 OH, 1,698 IN, 1,167 MN, 199 MO, 5,129 KY, 91 Nebraska; 2,715 MO six months. (The page labels the two columns only by the footnote on p.383; row labels via the mirror's run-on text, so each pair is as extracted.)

### (c) The ninety-day men: expiry dates

Arithmetic first: three months from muster-in. The 15 April call set service "for three months unless sooner discharged" (OR III-1 p.68); regiments mustered 18-30 April therefore expired roughly 18-30 July, before the 31 July checkpoint. This is inferred from the terms, not stated in one document.

Primary statements about expiring terms (read in this run):

| Date | Author, place | Statement | Source (read) | Quality |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 17 Jul 1861 | Maj-Gen Robert Patterson to Col. E. D. Townsend, Charlestown, Va. | Term of service of the Pennsylvania troops expires "within seven days" beginning the next day; 18 Pa regiments; can rely on none of them renewing | spirit61.info mirror of OR I-2 (page number not shown): https://spirit61.info/primary-sources/july-17-report-of-maj-gen-robert-patterson/ | return (mirror of OR) |
| 20 Jul 1861 | McDowell to Townsend, Centreville | Many thousands of his best men will leave within a few days; 4th Pennsylvania was leaving that day, McDowell had asked it to stay a few more days | OR I-2 p.308 via civilwar.com mirror (paraphrase by the fetch tool; wording not verified) | return, paraphrase |
| 21 Jul 1861 | Patterson to Townsend, Harpers Ferry | Terms expire in a few days; five regiments already gone home, four more leaving next day; remaining: Third Wisconsin, Second Massachusetts, Fourth Connecticut, 12th/19th/20th New York, etc. | https://spirit61.info/primary-sources/july-21-report-of-maj-gen-robert-patterson/ | return (mirror of OR) |
| 20-21 Jul 1861 (report written later) | McDowell's report on Bull Run | Large part of his force three-months volunteers, "terms of service were about expiring"; on the eve of battle the 4th Pennsylvania and the battery of Volunteer Artillery of the 8th New York Militia, whose term expired, insisted on discharge and moved to the rear | OR I-2 p.325 via civilwar.com mirror (https://civilwar.com/official-record/570-first-manassas/151576-325-series-i-volume-ii-serial-2-first-manassas.html); fetch tool paraphrase, not verified verbatim | return, paraphrase |
| 25 Jul 1861 | Patterson General Orders No. 33 | Term of the Pennsylvania troops having expired and nearly all gone home, Patterson relinquishes command (search-result snippet of the spirit61 page; I did not fetch this page) | https://spirit61.info/primary-sources/july-25-maj-gen-robert-patterson-general-orders-no-33/ | snippet-unverified |
| 8 May 1861 | Cameron to Curtin | asks that three-month Pennsylvania regiments not yet sent forward be remustered for three years or the war | OR III-1 p.178 | return |
| 1 Jul 1861 | War Dept report to the President | 80,000 three-month volunteers to be deducted from 310,000 | OR III-1 p.304 | return |

Dated regiment examples (secondary mirrors citing Dyer/Bates; the mirror is the only source I read for the dates):
- 3rd Pennsylvania: organised Harrisburg 20 Apr 1861, mustered out Harrisburg 29 Jul 1861 (pa-roots.com, cites Bates and Dyer): https://www.pa-roots.com/pacw/infantry/3rd/3rdorg.html
- 23rd Pennsylvania: mustered in Philadelphia 21 Apr, out 31 Jul 1861 (Bates: reached Philadelphia 29 Jul): https://www.pa-roots.com/pacw/infantry/23rd/23dorg.html
- Patterson's Pennsylvania regiments leaving the column, dates the units arrived at Harrisburg or Philadelphia for muster-out (civilwarintheeast.com, no source cited): 6th 18 Jul; 23rd 20 Jul; 9th, 13th, 14th, 16th 21 Jul; 7th, 8th 22 Jul; 1st, 2nd, 3rd, 10th, 17th, parts of 25th 23 Jul; 20th 24 Jul; 15th 25 Jul; 11th 27 Jul; 21st 31 Jul; 24th early Aug; 11th Indiana to Indianapolis 29 Jul: https://civilwarintheeast.com/?p=6388. These dates conflict with the pa-roots dates for the 3rd (23 Jul arrival vs 29 Jul muster-out): the mirror gives travel dates, pa-roots gives muster-out dates.
- Search-result snippets (not fetched, unverified): 1st Pa returned to Harrisburg 24 Jul; 14th Pa muster-out 7 Aug; 24th Pa 10 Aug; 25th Pa 1 Aug (with Wikipedia giving 26 Jul); Erie Regt 25 Jul.
- Ohio at Bull Run (Schenck's brigade, Tyler's division): 2nd Ohio organised Columbus 18 Apr 1861, mustered out 31 Jul 1861, fought 21 Jul (civilwarintheeast.com, no source: https://civilwarintheeast.com/us-regiments-batteries/ohio/2nd-ohio-infantry/); 1st Ohio mustered out 2 Aug 1861, three months (researchonline.net, which cites Dyer: https://researchonline.net/ohcw/unit27.htm). Both secondary; the two Ohio regiments were thus in the field past the nominal 90 days and left Washington's front within days of the battle.
- New York at Bull Run: 8th New York Militia's volunteer artillery battery insisted on discharge 20-21 Jul (McDowell report above). Other New York, Rhode Island and Massachusetts three-month units were not checked in this run (no dated muster-out source read): `needs-source`.

How many three-month regiments were still in service on 31 July: not found as a count. Bounding evidence: Cameron's 1 Jul report treated ~80,000 three-month men as about to leave; Patterson (21 Jul) and McDowell (20 Jul) show the first departures 18-25 Jul; the Pennsylvania examples above show muster-out between 20 Jul and 10 Aug (3rd PA 29 Jul; 23rd PA 31 Jul; 24th PA about 10 Aug; Ohio 1st 2 Aug). So on 31 July a minority of the three-month regiments (those that had overstayed or been mustered late: some Pa, 1st Ohio, parts of New York and Rhode Island) were still on the rolls, and most had gone home. This is an inference.

### (d) Conflicts

1. **Quota totals.** OR III-1 p.69 prints 94 regiments, 3,549 officers etc., 69,842 men, aggregate 73,391 (my row sum: 94 regiments; aggregate sums to 73,391 only with Pennsylvania at 12,500, so the printed "12,280" for PA is an OCR error; New York "2,631" men is "12,631"). The same 94/73,391 is repeated in OR III-1 p.303 (Secretary of War's report of 1 Jul 1861). The schenectadyhistory.org transcription (https://www.schenectadyhistory.org/military/civilwar/mr/militia_quota.html) gives the same 94 / 73,391 and the corrected cells (NY 12,631; PA 12,500). I could not find any source printing 73,910 or 72,540, nor a "93 regiments" total; a search for those numbers returned only the 75,000 aggregate. A reduction of Pennsylvania from 16 to 14 regiments (16 Apr, p.77) would make the table 92 regiments, and the p.77 figure of 8,960 privates is 14 x 640. `needs-source` for 73,910 and 72,540.
2. **Pennsylvania quota.** 16 regiments (p.69 table) versus 14 regiments (p.69 footnote, p.77 telegram of 16 Apr); a search result also stated 15 for Pennsylvania in a newspaper reprint (not read).
3. **Men furnished under the 15 April call.** 77,875 "immediately obtained" (p.700 text, and the sum of my 19 p.699 rows), 77,872 (p.699 printed total), "at least 80,000" (p.303/304, 1 Jul), 91,816 "final adjustment of credits" (fn p.69, repeated on p.303 and p.700). The p.69 footnote's state figures I read sum to 90,501 without Delaware; 91,816 would need Delaware = 1,315, but the footnote prints "7754" for Delaware and p.699 prints 775: one of my reads (a fetch-tool transcription) is wrong or the footnote is corrupt; `needs-source` (read the printed page).
4. **Ohio three-month strength.** Dennison on 27 Oct: 22 infantry regiments, 22,240 men (p.603); Cameron's table: 10,236 (p.699); fn p.69: 12,357. Dennison's count includes the extra regiments Ohio raised beyond its 13-regiment quota (see Carrington, p.104: 13th and 14th regts raised by 23 Apr, 6-8 beyond quota); the other two figures count what was credited.
5. **Indiana three-month men.** 5,196 (p.601, 6 regts at 866) versus 4,686 (p.699, fn p.69); difference 510.
6. **Missouri.** four regts "over 4,500" (p.794), 11,445 about 12 regts (p.303), 9,356 (p.699), 10,591 (fn).
7. **Act of 22/25 July.** OR p.380-383 reads the 22 July act as up to 500,000 volunteers, "not exceeding three" (years) and "not less than six months"; p.383 reads the 25 July act as another 500,000. A secondary source (Appletons' text on civilwarencyclopedia.org, search snippet only) gives 600,000. The Statutes at Large page (12 Stat. 268) is a scan I could not read. The p.700 sentence ("states ... 500,000 volunteers serving three years or the war's duration") agrees with 500,000. Statutes need a direct read.
8. **Date of Cameron's 16 Apr rendezvous telegram to six governors** ("20 May", p.75) is a fetch-tool paraphrase; verify.
9. **Terms.** The 3 May proclamation says three years (p.146); New York mustered "two years" regiments under its own act of 16 Apr (p.178 mention of the state act; p.288 "33 mustered for two years"); the Act of 22 July says 6 months to 3 years. The p.384 footnote counts 30,950 New York two-year men.

### Gaps

- **Statutes at Large** 12 Stat. 268 (ch. 9, 22 Jul 1861) and the 25 Jul act: govinfo PDF is a scanned image (https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/STATUTE-12/pdf/STATUTE-12-Pg268.pdf) and dheller.org PDF (https://dheller.org/Timeline-files/US-Statutes/v12/c37/s1/pub/ch9-p268.pdf) is JBIG2 scan; I could not extract text. Also the 6 Aug 1861 ratification act (12 Stat. 326) not read; OR p.383 footnote used instead.
- **Richardson / Lincoln 4 July message**: avalon.law.yale.edu 404; teachingamericanhistory.org 403; the text was read at https://millercenter.org/node/2092 only (reputable transcription, not Richardson). It does not contain the 42,034 / 18,000 numbers (those are in the proclamation of 3 May).
- **Dyer's Compendium**: archive.org text download returned 404 / timed out (https://archive.org/stream/08697590.3359.emory.edu/08697590.3359.emory.edu_djvu.txt is a 14 MB file; fetch tool saw only the item page); generalstaff.org (https://generalstaff.org/WFA/WFA.htm and /WFA/Dyers/OH_Inf_US.htm) returned 403 (not routed around). Regiment-by-regiment muster dates and the count of three-year regiments by state at 31 July are therefore `needs-source`.
- **Phisterer, Fox, Hesseltine** (*Lincoln and the War Governors*, 1948): not read in this run; no page citations given for any of them.
- Delaware, Maryland, Kansas, California, Oregon governors and responses: not read in OR III-1 pages 67-104 or 284-291; Oregon appears nowhere in the tables I read.
- OR III-1 pp.79-84, 86-94, 96-103 and 105-116 (April-May governors' correspondence), and pp.146-177 (May proclamation follow-ups, regimental acceptances) not read.
- The Pennsylvania 28 Oct reply is "Not found" in OR (p.599 note); Pennsylvania's three-year count at 21 Jun and 31 Jul not obtained.
- McDowell's and Patterson's complete OR I-2 page numbers: the spirit61 mirrors show no page numbers; civilwar.com page ids 151409-151425 (OR I-2 pp.158-174) held Patterson's reports but I read only the spirit61 text.

### Findings outside scope

- OR III-1 p.303-305 is the Secretary of War's report to the President of 1 July 1861 (153 regiments in service plus 55 accepted; 310,000 at the government's command; 230,000 after the three-month men leave): useful for the 31 July checkpoint force strength, and for N5 (strengths).
- OR III-1 p.699 (Cameron, 1 Dec 1861) gives the full state-by-state "for the war" estimates (December, not 31 July) and arm-of-service totals (infantry 557,208 volunteers etc.); the sum of rows for the three-months' column is 77,875.
- OR III-1 p.288: Ohio's quota stated as 21 three-year regiments on 21 June, a figure for how the 3 May call was apportioned (the 42,034 / 40 regiments).
- Minnesota's 1st Regiment changed its term from three months to the war in place on 29 May (p.604): a mechanism (remustering a three-month unit) the game might want.
- New York's two-year volunteers, raised under a state act of 16 April 1861 (p.178, p.288): a distinct "state-service" term.
- The 22 June replies show Cameron's single telegram of 21 June is the only wide census of regiments before the checkpoint; useful for a hidden-stat design (the lead learns counts only by asking the governors).
- Fetch-tool reliability: several pages' paraphrases were visibly wrong in small ways (garbled place names, a signer misattributed); I used corroborating pages where possible and flagged the rest. All figures in this file are as extracted and should be re-read against the printed pages before being used as hard data.


## Appendix G: The Confederate calls and state forces (helper N7)


### Method and reliability (read first)

- The shell had no network in this run, so every page was read through the WebFetch tool. It summarises each 100,000-character chunk of an OCR text: **page numbers are the running heads as the tool read them (can drift by one), quotations are NOT verbatim unless marked "exact"**, and numbers are copied by the tool from OCR. Items tagged `[verify]` should be re-read against the scan before they carry weight.
- **OR IV-1** (Series IV vol. 1, Confederate Authorities, serial 127), read as Cornell OCR full text: `archive.org/details/cu31924079579524`; text read at `dn790003.ca.archive.org/0/items/cu31924079579524/cu31924079579524_djvu.txt` (2,341,743 chars; chars 0-2,000,000 = pp. 1-524, i.e. to 1 Aug 1861). Cited as OR IV-1 p.NNN.
- **JCCS** = Journal of the Congress of the Confederate States, vol. 1 (Senate Doc. 234, 58th Cong. 2d sess., 1904), read at `dn760008.eu.archive.org/0/items/journalofcongres00conf/journalofcongres00conf_djvu.txt` (chars 200,000-800,000 sampled: pp. 53-203, 15 Feb-9 May 1861).
- **Digest** = *Digest of the Military and Naval Laws of the Confederate States* (`archive.org/download/digestofmilitary00inconf/...`; compiler not verified in this run), read chars 0-200,000. Secondary compilation of the statutes; gives margin dates and chapters.
- **OR I-2, I-3, I-4, III-5** pages read on `civilwar.com/official-record/...` (page transcriptions).
- Richardson vol. 1-2 was **not read** (could not download; Davis's 29 April message was read in OR IV-1 instead). Statutes at Large of the CSA not read; the Digest and OR IV-1 print the acts.
- Quality tags: `act` = printed statute/ordinance; `letter` = dated official correspondence; `return` = dated count; `estimate`; `secondary` = encyclopedia or similar, a lead only; `snippet-unverified`.
